
## Lizzie's heavy machine-gun mount (lzMount / "HEAVY MG") — fire cadence

### [UNVERIFIED] Mike wants Lizzie's machine-gun attachment to fire 10% slower. The attachment is the HEAVY MG vehicle mount (lzMount) that flies in on an arc, shrinks and docks at her nose, then fires twin-barrel heavy slugs in place of her primary for 15 seconds. Its entire fire cadence lives in one constant.
- where: game.js:23653
- now: `const LZ_SLUG_CD = 0.06875;` is the seconds-between-volleys interval for the docked mount, and it is the single owner of that trigger. `_weaponCadence()` (assets/game.js:20138) early-returns `LZ_SLUG_CD` the moment `lzMountActive()` is true, short-circuiting the whole WEAPON_CADENCE table and level ramp. The caller at assets/game.js:26062 then does `player.fireCd=(cd!=null?cd:0.2)*(1-(PILOTMOD?PILOTMOD.fire:0))` — Lizzie's stat card is `fire:0.08` (assets/game.js:7126), so the effective interval is 0.06875 x 0.92 = 0.063250s (~15.81 volleys/sec, two slugs per volley). That pilot modifier is MULTIPLICATIVE, so scaling the constant scales the real-world rate by exactly the same percentage. The slug push site in `pShoot()` (assets/game.js:21582) carries no cooldown of its own — the comment directly above it at line 21578 records that a former fixed-1/60 cooldown there was itself the bug ("_weaponCadence already owns this trigger"), so line 23653 is the only place to edit. The house convention for a percentage change here is applied to the INTERVAL, not the rate: the comment at assets/game.js:23651 documents the last pass as "+25% between twin-barrel beats", implemented as 0.055 x 1.25 = 0.06875.
- FIX: Single-token edit in the constant block for Lizzie's mount: at assets/game.js:23653 change `const LZ_SLUG_CD  = 0.06875;` to `const LZ_SLUG_CD  = 0.075625;` (0.06875 x 1.10). That takes the effective cadence from 0.063250s to 0.069575s, i.e. 15.81 -> 14.37 volleys/sec. Touch nothing else — leave LZ_SLUG_DMG (7, assets/game.js:23638), LZ_SLUG_SPD (7.875, assets/game.js:23654) and LZ_LIFE (15, assets/game.js:23658) alone; the long comment at 23639-23650 explicitly records that damage was measured for the one-or-two-shot brief and is not the lever. Follow local style and append a short note to the 23651 comment recording the -10% pass. Note on interpretation: if Mike means "rate x 0.9" rather than "interval +10%", the value would be 0.06875/0.9 = 0.0763889 instead; the 0831 precedent at line 23651 favours the interval reading (0.075625).
- risk: Low gameplay risk but ONE test will fail unless updated with it — see the second finding. Directionally safe: the 0810f comment at assets/game.js:23639 warns this constant was once 0.16 and Mike called it "way too slow"; 0.075625 is still less than half that and remains far faster than the ~0.20s primary it replaces, so it does not regress into the complaint. DPS over the 15s window drops ~9% (about 221 -> 201 dmg/sec at 7 dmg x 2 barrels), which may push stage-8 fodder time-to-kill up slightly, but the shots-to-kill table in the 23626-23637 comment is a function of LZ_SLUG_DMG only and is unaffected. The `lzslug` bullets are unchanged, so no collision, art or audio path is touched.

### [UNVERIFIED] A QA harness hard-pins the exact cadence number, so changing LZ_SLUG_CD alone will make that check report failure even though the change is intended.
- where: qa_combat_balance_0831.js:209
- now: The pass/fail expression asserts `Math.abs(audit.lizzieCadence-.06875)<.00001`, where `audit.lizzieCadence` is sampled straight off the live constant at _BUILD_SOURCE/qa_combat_balance_0831.js:59 (`lizzieCadence:LZ_SLUG_CD`). It is an equality pin with a 1e-5 tolerance, not a bound, so any retune of the constant trips it. The other three checks that read this constant are one-sided bounds and all still pass at 0.075625: _BUILD_SOURCE/test_fl.js:7782 (`LZ_SLUG_CD < 0.12`), _BUILD_SOURCE/test_fl.js:8251 (`LZ_SLUG_CD<0.12 && LZ_SLUG_DMG>=6`), and _BUILD_SOURCE/probe_weapons.py:185 (`LZ_SLUG_CD < 0.16`).
- FIX: In the same commit, update the pinned literal at _BUILD_SOURCE/qa_combat_balance_0831.js:209 from `.06875` to `.075625`, leaving the `<.00001` tolerance and the adjacent `lizzieSpeed-7.875` pin untouched. No change is needed to test_fl.js or probe_weapons.py.
- risk: None to the game — this file is a QA harness, not shipped code. The only risk is the opposite one: skipping this edit leaves a red check that looks like a real regression and may prompt someone to revert the intended tuning.

## Player ship draw-scale is inconsistent across every stage-entry path: the entry cinematics settle the hull onto a stale 76-82px "pose" height while PLAY blits it at a flat 60px, so the ship visibly inflates during the settle and then snaps back down on GO.

### [UNVERIFIED] On every level, the ship pops DOWN in size at the instant the entrance hands over to gameplay (the "then down").
- where: game.js:54019
- now: playShipPose() is the single "pose PLAY starts from" that all three entry cinematics settle onto, and its h is still the OLD formula `((player.h||34)*2.05)/cf`. player.h is 30 (line 7896) and never reassigned, so that yields a CANVAS height of 61.5/cf = 76.1px for axel/cole/freezer, 77.5 decker, 77.6 falva, 77.2 juggernaut, 73.0 lizzie, 76.7 maverick, 82.5 yuri. PLAY does NOT draw that: _drawPlayerCore blits the hull at `const h=SHIP_DRAW_H` = 60 (line 31341, constant at line 30895), the same flat 60 for every pilot. So GO shrinks the ship 21% (27% on Yuri). The file already records this exact formula as wrong: the 0819c note at line 30950 says `(player.h||34)*2.05` was "a second opinion about how big the ship is, and it was 43% over the truth" and moved PLAY onto SHIP_DRAW_H — but playShipPose was left behind, silently re-opening the seam drop 0810a had closed. 0810a's own comment (line 54005-54010) claims "Both sides read this now", and that claim went stale when 0819c changed PLAY's side. It is also pilot-inconsistent: the entry size varies 73-82.5px by pilot while PLAY is 60 for all nine.
- FIX: In playShipPose() (line 54012), change the returned `h` to the value PLAY actually blits: `h: SHIP_DRAW_H`. Leave x and y alone. `cf` (line 54014) then becomes unused and can be dropped. This single edit fixes GS.LAUNCH, GS.WARPENTRY and any other consumer of the pose at once.
- risk: The launch's plume block (line 54373) sizes itself as `shipH*1.15`, so the transition flame gets ~21% smaller — the comment at 54364-54380 records three failed attempts to unify that plume and warns it is the only visible flame at launch scale, so eyeball it after the change. drawL78Entry (55908) and the outbound pull-away (56073) also read pose.h and will start/end from 60 instead of 76.

### [UNVERIFIED] The ship visibly grows during the settle beat just before 3-2-1 (the "scale us up") on every ground stage.
- where: game.js:54324
- now: drawLaunch's 'settle' phase runs `shipH=lerp(62, POSE.h, k)` over 0.45s, i.e. 62 -> 76.1 canvas px, a 23% inflation. The countdown phase then holds `shipH=POSE.h` (line 54328) for the whole 3-2-1-GO, and PLAY takes over at 60. Full ground-stage trace: run 62 (line 54301), brake 62, settle 62->76.1, cd 76.1, PLAY 60. That is literally up-then-down, and the 62 it starts from matches neither constant.
- FIX: Once playShipPose returns SHIP_DRAW_H, this lerp becomes 62->60 — still a visible 3% twitch. Make it a no-op by changing the initializer at line 54301 from `shipH=62` to `shipH=SHIP_DRAW_H`; the settle lerp at 54324 then reads lerp(SHIP_DRAW_H, POSE.h, k) with both ends equal. Do not delete the lerp — the space branches still overwrite shipH before it.
- risk: The run/brake ship is 2px smaller against the entry connector art; nothing else keys off the literal 62. If line 54301 is changed, the two remaining literal 62s at line 54311 (space lift-off) must be checked, since that branch lerps from 62 as its floor.

### [UNVERIFIED] Stages 5 and 9 pump the size hardest: the ship doubles on the climb, halves on the brake, then grows again on the settle.
- where: game.js:54311
- now: For _space stages the run phase does `shipH=lerp(62, 128, lift)` (line 54311; stage 9 does lerp(14, SPACE_SHIP_SIZE=48) at line 54309), then brake does `shipH=lerp(128, SHIP_DRAW_H, k)` (line 54316) taking it back to 60 — and then the unconditional settle at line 54324 restarts from the literal 62 and climbs to 76.1 again, ignoring the 60 the brake just arrived at. Net for stage 5: 62 -> 128 -> 60 -> 62 -> 76.1 -> 60 at GO. Four direction changes in one entrance.
- FIX: If Mike wants one size everywhere, drop the height lerps and keep only the Y movement: at line 54311 remove `shipH=lerp(62,128,lift);` (keep the shipY lerp), at line 54309 remove `shipH=lerp(14,SPACE_SHIP_SIZE,pe);` (keep shipY), and delete the `if(_space) shipH=lerp(...)` at line 54316. shipH then stays at SHIP_DRAW_H through the whole space entry.
- risk: The 128px climb reads as deliberate staging ("the ship scales up as it rockets toward space", line 54310) and stage 9's 14->48 is the portal emergence — removing them flattens an intentional beat, so this is a product call, not purely a bug. Also _gravityDrawSize (line 54331) is `Math.max(SPACE_SHIP_SIZE, shipH)`, so with shipH pinned at 60 the Gravity Mode craft draws at a constant 60 instead of tracking the climb.

### [UNVERIFIED] Level 1's own opening cinematic has the same landing size, so stage 1 shrinks at GO exactly like the others.
- where: game.js:50453
- now: openingDrawShip computes its own size instead of asking playShipPose: `const dh=((player.h||34)*2.05/cf)*O.shipScale` — the same stale 76.1px base, times O.shipScale. shipScale is 1 on the runway, falls to 0.38 across TAKEOFF (line 50265), returns to 1 through SKY (50272) and is pinned at 1 for COAST and HANDOFF (50277, 50279). So level 1 draws 76.1 -> 28.9 -> 76.1 and then PLAY blits 60. Fixing playShipPose alone does NOT fix stage 1, because this call site never reads it.
- FIX: In openingDrawShip, change line 50453 to `const dh=SHIP_DRAW_H*O.shipScale;` and drop the now-unused `cf` on line 50452. Leave the shipScale curve alone — the 1 -> 0.38 -> 1 climb-away is the authored takeoff, and with this base it lands on exactly the size PLAY draws.
- risk: The whole level-1 takeoff reads ~21% smaller against the runway plate; the pan (O.panY) and player.x/y are untouched so nothing moves. If the takeoff now looks undersized, adjust the shipScale curve rather than reintroducing a second size formula.

### [UNVERIFIED] The stage 7 -> 8 warp entrance settles onto the same oversized pose before shrinking at GO.
- where: game.js:55908
- now: drawL78Entry (GS.WARPENTRY, dispatched at line 39473) draws the ship with `sh=lerp(6, pose.h, k)` where pose=playShipPose() — 6px at the rift mouth growing to 76.1px over 2.48s, then GS.PLAY blits 60. Same 21% drop on GO as every other stage; recorded here because it is a separate entry path from drawLaunch and confirms the defect is in the shared pose, not in one cinematic.
- FIX: No edit needed here. Fixing playShipPose (line 54019) to return SHIP_DRAW_H corrects this call site automatically, since it already reads the shared pose.
- risk: None specific — this site is only affected via the pose fix. Verify the 6px -> 60px growth still reads as emerging from the rift rather than starting too close to final size.

## Spread level 4 renders black because the shared WLV_BODY palette-swap table maps tier 4 to a 'black' multiply, and the spread draw path (unlike the machine gun) has nothing in front of it to win first

### [UNVERIFIED] The player SPREAD weapon (weapon index 1) at level 4 fires a near-black round instead of the white one Mike's tier scheme calls for (lv1 orange, lv2 blue, lv3 green, lv4 WHITE, lv5 red).
- where: game.js:36844
- now: pShoot() at line 21665 pushes each spread pellet as {kind:'spread', lv} where lv is run.wlevel verbatim (line 21561: `const w=run.weapon, lv=run.wlevel;`), so "lvl 4" is literally b.lv===4. drawBullets' spread branch at line 37930-37931 tries the nca_87 pack FIRST and returns on success: `p87Draw(_p.rc, b.x, b.y, _p.res, 36+clamp(b.lv||1,1,8)*1.4, b.lv, wlvGlow(b.lv), 1)`. p87Draw calls p87Body(lv) (line 36859-36861), which reads `WLV_BODY[clamp(lv|0||1,1,8)]` (line 36853) and, for a non-null mode, returns `xartPalette(P87_SHEET, m)` (line 36855). WLV_BODY at line 36844 is `{1:'#8a5a2b', 2:'#c8d2dc', 3:'white', 4:'black', ...}` — tier 4's BODY is 'black'. xartPalette's black branch (lines 39269-39270) is `globalCompositeOperation='multiply'; fillStyle='#2a2b31'`, i.e. every pixel scaled to ~16-19% brightness. I measured the actual nca_87 pose cells the spread uses (row 3, cols 0/1/2 of assets/game/atlas/nca_87.png, 192px pitch): mean opaque ink is (183,197,194) — a light silver-white round — and after the #2a2b31 multiply it becomes (30,33,37), i.e. black. So the round is black by construction, not by a missing asset. Two independent confirmations that white is the intent: WLV_GLOW[4] is '#dfe8ff' (line 36842), a white halo, and the fallback slug the spread would otherwise use, nmgv_4, is documented at line 37962 as '#e5dbdb white'. The 'black' value is not a typo — the drop-0812d comment at lines 36772-36777 quotes Mike giving two lists at once, a tier list ("lvl 4 white") and a body list ("level 4 could be black"), and the body list is what got coded. Mike's new report is that the body list is wrong for tier 4.
- FIX: In WLV_BODY at assets/game.js line 36844, change `4:'black'` to `4:'white'`. One token. Do not touch WLV_GLOW — its '#dfe8ff' at tier 4 is already correct, and xartPalette already special-cases 'white' (line 39271-39274: a 'color' pass plus a 0.34 screen lift) so it will not desaturate to grey the way a hue swap would.
- risk: WLV_BODY is shared, so two side effects. (1) The player muzzle flash also goes through p87Draw (line 31209, gated on run.weapon===0||1), so the level-4 muzzle flash turns white too — consistent with the tier, but it is a visible change Mike did not ask for. (2) WLV_BODY[3] is already 'white', so levels 3 and 4 would share a body and be told apart only by their glow (green vs white); if that reads as a regression, the follow-up is to give tier 3 a green-tinted body rather than to revert tier 4. Cole's tiers 6-8 and machine-gun levels 1-5 are unaffected (see the next finding).

### [UNVERIFIED] Only the SPREAD looks black at level 4 — the machine gun at the same level looks correctly white — which is why the bug reads as a spread-specific defect rather than a shared palette problem.
- where: game.js:37872
- now: The two branches of drawBullets are not symmetric in what they try first. The mg branch at line 37871-37872 does `const _cLv=b.lv||1; if(_cLv<=5 && mgcfDraw(b,_cLv)) continue;` — mgcfDraw (line 36828-36832) draws the authored per-level plate mgcf_<lv>_<frame> with tint null, and those plates are already colour-graded on disk: I measured assets/game/fx_0825/mg_bullet_4_3.png at a mean opaque (255,255,255) pure white and mg_bullet_3_3.png at (102,248,133) green. So for levels 1-5 the machine gun returns before ever reaching p87Draw, and WLV_BODY[4]='black' can never touch it. The spread branch at line 37930 has no mgcfDraw guard at all — p87Draw is the first thing it tries, so the black body swap lands on the spread and nothing else at levels 1-5. (Only Cole's exclusive MG tiers 6-8 reach p87Draw on the machine gun, and those use WLV_BODY 6/7/8.) This is the same 'first path in a fallback chain wins' trap the file already documents twice for this exact code — lines 37831-37843 for nmg_ shadowing the coloured MG art, and lines 37861-37866 warning that nca_87 must go first or be unreachable.
- FIX: No second edit is needed if the WLV_BODY[4] fix above is applied — this finding is the explanation for why the symptom is spread-only, and is the reason a fix aimed at the machine gun would appear to do nothing. If Mike instead wants the spread to use the same authored per-level plates the machine gun uses (which would make all five tiers match exactly rather than approximately), the alternative one-line change is to add `if((b.lv||1)<=5 && mgcfDraw(b,(b.lv||1))) continue;` immediately before line 37930 — but that swaps the spread's authored ±46.2 degree diagonal poses for a single non-rotated pellet, so it is the larger change and I do not recommend it for this report.
- risk: None if left alone. If the alternative mgcfDraw route is taken instead, every spread pellet loses its authored diagonal pose (P87_POSE, lines 36808-36817) and its per-shot rotation, so a wide fan at level 5 would draw seven identical upright pellets travelling sideways to their own flight lines — the exact failure the comment at lines 37940-37947 warns about.

## FALVA — one orbiting laser helper that fires a spread

### [UNVERIFIED] Falva gets TWO laser helpers, not one. Mike wants one.
- where: game.js:22619
- now: falvaLasersStart() (line 22618) hard-codes a two-element array into the module-level `falvaBalls`: `{side:-1, x:player.x-FALVA_BALL_OFFX, ...}` on 22620 and `{side:+1, ...}` on 22621. Nothing else ever pushes or pops from falvaBalls — every other site (falvaLasersUpdate 22629, drawFalvaBalls 22649, endSpecial 22576) just iterates or clears it. So the count is set in exactly one place. startSpecial calls falvaLasersStart at line 22548 (its trailing comment still says '2 balls anchor to her sides').
- FIX: In falvaLasersStart (assets/game.js:22618-22624), delete one of the two literals so the array holds a single ball. Keep the surviving entry's `side` property defined (see the drawFalvaBalls finding) — set it to +1. Also update the stale trailing comment on line 22548 and the plural floatText 'LASER BALLS!' on line 22623.
- risk: Halves the helper's straight-laser DPS on its own (2 balls x 2dmg every 0.10s = 40 dps -> 20 dps) before the spread change adds it back. Tests in _BUILD_SOURCE/test_fl.js assert length===2 (see separate finding).

### [UNVERIFIED] The helper is side-anchored with a bob, not orbiting. It sits at a fixed left/right offset from the hull.
- where: game.js:22633
- now: falvaLasersUpdate computes the target position as a static side offset: `const tx=player.x + bl.side*FALVA_BALL_OFFX;` (22633) and `const ty=player.y + FALVA_BALL_OFFY + Math.sin(bl.bob)*3;` (22634), then smooth-follows with `bl.x += (tx-bl.x)*Math.min(1, dt*18)` (22635-22636). FALVA_BALL_OFFX=30 / FALVA_BALL_OFFY=4 are declared at 22616-22617. `bl.bob+=dt*6` (22632) only produces a 3px vertical wobble — there is no angular term anywhere, so the ball can never circle her.
- FIX: In falvaLasersUpdate (assets/game.js:22625-22645), replace lines 22633-22634 with Axel's orbit formula: `const a=performance.now()/440; const tx=player.x+Math.cos(a)*FALVA_BALL_R; const ty=player.y+Math.sin(a)*FALVA_BALL_R;` and add `const FALVA_BALL_R=52;` next to the existing offsets at 22616. Keep the smooth-follow on 22635-22636 (or set bl.x/bl.y directly for a rigid orbit like Axel's). FALVA_BALL_OFFX/OFFY then become unused.
- risk: An orbiting ball passes BELOW and BESIDE the player, so a straight-up bolt spawned at bl.y-10 will sometimes originate behind her; with the spread change the fan direction should stay -PI/2-centred (or aim), not follow the orbit angle, or half the shots fly downward.

### [UNVERIFIED] Reference implementation: Axel's helper orbit, which Mike is naming as the model.
- where: game.js:30055
- now: axelBallPos() at 30055-30059 is the whole orbit: `const a = performance.now()/440;` then `x: player.x + Math.cos(a)*AXEL_BALL_R, y: player.y + Math.sin(a)*AXEL_BALL_R`. AXEL_BALL_R=52 (30050), AXEL_BALL_CD=0.62 (30051). The 0801fh comment at 30033-30049 states the design intent explicitly — the /440 clock and R=52 are chosen so the helper matches the aegis ring's angular rate and direction while sitting OUTSIDE it; the aegis orbs use the identical `now/440 + i*TAU/n` at R=30 on line 31258. axelBallTick (30060-30082) picks the nearest live enemy ahead and fires one 'axbeam'; axelBallDraw (30107-30130) rotates the sprite by `P.a*1.6` and breathes the scale with `30+Math.sin(now/120+P.a)*2` — copied from drawFalvaBalls, so the two draws are already siblings.

- risk: None (read-only reference). If Falva reuses the exact `performance.now()/440` phase, her ball and Axel's sit at the same angle — harmless, they never coexist.

### [UNVERIFIED] The helper only fires a single straight bolt. The spread was deliberately deleted in drop 0724cf and Mike is now asking for it back.
- where: game.js:22641
- now: The whole firing path is lines 22638-22643: `bl.fireCd-=dt; if(bl.fireCd<=0){ bl.fireCd=0.10; pBullets.push({x:bl.x, y:bl.y-10, vx:0, vy:-13, w:8, h:22, dmg:2, kind:'flaser', t:0, _f:(Math.random()*8)|0}); ... }`. vx is hard 0, so it is a pure vertical bolt. Keep speed 13 to match the current bolt. Raise bl.fireCd from 0.10 if the DPS is too high.

Also triples pBullets churn during a 15s special; the perf note in CLAUDE.md (drawBullets at 14.63ms) is about shadowBlur per bullet, and the flaser draw at 37707 sets shadowBlur every frame.

### [UNVERIFIED] Mike asked for '45 degree angle laser frames via flip/turn, flip for the other side' — but NO new or flipped art frames are needed. The renderer already rotates the laser sprite to its velocity.
- where: game.js:37703
- now: The flaser draw branch at 37698-37712 computes `const ang=Math.atan2(b.vy||-1, b.vx||0)+Math.PI/2;` on 37703 and does `ctx.translate(b.x,b.y); ctx.rotate(ang);` on 37705 before drawing fllaser_<n>. So any non-zero vx already renders the beam pointing along its flight line, left or right, at any angle. The flspread branch does the same on 37717. The 37700 comment explains _f: the 8 frames are SHAPES chosen once at spawn, not an animation reel ('the GLOW animates, the sprite does not') — so a fan of bolts with random _f is exactly how the system is meant to be used. axbeam at 37160 uses the identical rotate-by-_ang.

- risk: None. Baking rotated frames instead would double the atlas footprint and re-create the exact 'a second copy of her art that can drift from it' problem the 0810b comment at 30083-30101 warns about for Axel.

- FIX: Keep `side:+1` on the surviving ball literal in falvaLasersStart, or replace 22655 with a plain `ctx.rotate(now/240)`. Do not delete the field without changing this line.
- risk: Low. If you also want the sprite to counter-rotate against the orbit like Axel's (`ctx.rotate(P.a*1.6)` at 30115), swap the expression rather than dropping it.

## Stage-5 warp gate run: the dashed route connector, and the Shadow Orb's launch cue peaking 1.7s after the trigger

### [UNVERIFIED] Mike: "no lines connecting to the warp gates please, I dont need to travel in those lines, just travel through the gates before they disappear off screen."
- where: game.js:34993
- now: s5RunDraw() (defined at game.js:34988) strokes a dashed cyan polyline through the centre of every consecutive gate pair before it draws the gate sprites. The block is game.js:34991-34997: line 34993 sets `ctx.lineWidth=2; ctx.strokeStyle='rgba(105,228,255,.30)'; ctx.setLineDash([8,8])`, the loop at 34994-34996 walks `s5run.gates[i]` -> `s5run.gates[i+1]` doing `ctx.moveTo(a.x,a.y); ctx.lineTo(z.x,z.y)`, and 34997 strokes it. The comment above it at 34991-34992 states the intent explicitly - "The attached route is visible as one connected flight plan rather than eight unrelated circles" - so this is a deliberate addition, not a leftover debug overlay. That is exactly the "line to travel in" Mike is describing: gate centres are `x: W*S5R_ROUTE[i]` (s5RunInit, game.js:34803-34806) with the route `[.28,.54,.76,.23,.54,.29,.69,.18]` (game.js:34796), so the polyline zig-zags down the screen and reads as a rail the ship is meant to follow rather than eight gates to fly through. Corroboration that this is the only such line: `setLineDash` appears exactly ONCE in all 58,211 lines of game.js, at 34993. The only other `lineTo` calls anywhere near the gate code are game.js:35191 (riftReturnDraw) and game.js:35229 (s9MapDraw) and both are campaign-map cinematic streaks between map nodes, drawn in the MX/MY/S map transform - not in-flight gameplay. There is no player-to-gate tether anywhere. warpFxDraw (game.js:34968-34987) draws only screen-centred concentric rings and a slice-offset refraction, and its own comment at 34965-34967 confirms it "contains no speed-line substitute".
- FIX: In s5RunDraw() delete the seven-line route block, game.js:34991-34997 (the two comment lines plus `ctx.save()...ctx.stroke();ctx.restore();`). Nothing else references it: `_routeStarted` is declared and consumed entirely inside that block, and the dash/stroke state is wrapped in its own ctx.save()/ctx.restore(). The gate loop that follows at 34998 is untouched, so the eight gates, the pass/miss alpha, the last-gate glow and the pip counter all still draw. Do not touch s5RunForegroundDraw (game.js:35034) - it draws no line, only the clipped near rim of each gate.
- risk: Purely cosmetic removal. The only loss is the visual hint of where the next gate sits; the gates themselves stay visible because they spawn as a full convoy (all eight exist at once and drift down together - s5RunInit at game.js:34801-34808), so the player can still see and plan the shimmy. No gameplay path reads the line: the pass/miss test in s5RunTick (game.js:34893-34901) is a pure line-crossing check against `g.y`/`g.py` and `S5R_OPEN`, entirely independent of the drawn polyline. Zero risk of a blank-screen or state regression.

### [UNVERIFIED] Mike: "in space, the shadow orb, you can heard the sound clearly delayed for impact." A previous drop (0903q) already tried to fix this and fixed the wrong sample.
- where: game.js:57647
- now: The impact cue itself is NOT deferred and is not the problem - I traced and ruled that out. spaceShadowBlast (game.js:21481) calls spaceImpact (game.js:21436) synchronously on the collision frame from spaceBulletTick's `if(hit){b._blastPrimary=hit;spaceShadowBlast(b);}` (game.js:21555), and spaceImpact fires `S.spaceShadowHit` at game.js:21464-21468 with no timer. I measured the impact sample and drop 0903q's trim really did land: reviewed_shadow_orb_impact.wav peaks at 60.0 ms and hits 50% of peak at 4.1 ms - it is punchy and on time.

The delay is in the LAUNCH sample, which the 0903q comment measured and then walked past. game.js:57647 maps `spaceShadowRelease:'assets/game/sounds/reviewed_shadow_orb_launch.wav'`, fired by spaceShadowRelease() at game.js:21291 via `spaceWeaponCue('spaceShadowRelease','shadowRelease')` -> `Snd.play(pick,1.72)` (game.js:21188-21189). I measured that file: 2250 ms long, and its 100 ms RMS envelope is -26.4 dB at 0 ms, DIPS to -44.2 dB at 300 ms, then swells continuously to a -9.8 dB plateau held from 1400 ms to 1900 ms, with the absolute peak sample at 1744.5 ms. So the loudest event in the entire Shadow Orb sound arrives ~1.4-1.9 s after the trigger frame.

The orb has already hit by then. Its velocity is `-(4.15+lv*0.18+p*4.45)` px/frame (game.js:21284), i.e. 332-527 px/s at 60fps, over the ~200-330 px from the player's nose to the target band on a VH=512 screen (game.js:14) - a flight time of roughly 0.4-1.0 s. The player therefore hears: a faint tick at fire, a correct small crack at contact, and then a large delayed WHOOMP 0.7-1.3 s AFTER the visible impact.

And the late swell is louder than the real impact. Launch plays at `c01(1.72 * g1.00 * sfx0.42 * master1.0)` = 0.722; the impact plays at `c01(1 * g0.92 * 0.42 * 1.0)` = 0.386 (gains at game.js:57852-57853, volume math in A.play at game.js:57949). The delayed swell is ~+5.4 dB over the on-contact cue, which is why the ear assigns it as "the impact sound, late".

The 0903q comment at game.js:57648-57652 proves this was the near-miss: it correctly measured "a reviewed launch cue that does not reach -12 dB until 928 ms" (my measurement of that same file's first -12 dB crossing: 928.4 ms - an exact match), then cut the IMPACT sample instead of the launch one and left the launch untouched.
- FIX: Apply the 0903q treatment to the launch cue instead of the impact cue. Re-cut assets/game/sounds/reviewed_shadow_orb_launch.wav so its body starts at the plateau - cut at ~1340 ms (60 ms ahead of the -9.8 dB onset at 1400 ms), 4 ms fade-in, normalised - exactly as reviewed_shadow_orb_impact.wav was cut 60 ms ahead of explosion_plasma.wav's 454 ms peak. That drops the file to ~900 ms with its transient on the trigger frame and needs no code change if replaced in place. If you would rather not touch the asset, the code-only equivalent is the one-line swap at game.js:57647, re-pointing `spaceShadowRelease` at a front-loaded launch cue, and updating the comment block at 57648-57652 to record that the launch, not the impact, was the swell. Do not change spaceImpact (game.js:21436) or the spaceShadowHit mapping at game.js:57653 - both are already correct.
- risk: The launch cue loses its 1.4 s pre-roll swell, so a held full charge will sound less cinematic on release - the build-up is currently carried by the separate spaceShadowCharge loop (game.js:21313, nsp_bof2_charge_shot.mp3), which is unaffected, so the charge tension is preserved. Keep the original file on disk under its old name if you want to A/B it. If instead you only lower the launch gain (game.js:57852) rather than re-cutting, the boom lands late but quieter - it will still read as a delayed impact, so gain alone will not close this.

### [UNVERIFIED] Secondary contributor, specific to "in space": a duplicate copy of every sound effect is deliberately scheduled ~64-98 ms late while the eight-gate run is armed.
- where: game.js:57930
- now: A.play calls `A._warpSfx(a.src,a.volume)` unconditionally on every successful SFX play (game.js:57961). _warpSfx (game.js:57927-57931) returns early only when `A.warpMix<=.04`; otherwise line 57930 does `setTimeout(..., 64+mix*34)` and inside that timer constructs a brand-new `window.Audio()` on the same src at `playbackRate=.985` and `volume*mix*.22`. During the gate run warpMix is driven to `clamp(.10+s5run.idx/S5R_N*.78,0,1)` - up to 0.88 - by s5RunTick at game.js:34904-34905 and 34920, so the timer is ~94 ms and every cue, including the Shadow Orb impact, gets an audible slapback. The real lag is worse than 94 ms because a freshly constructed HTMLAudioElement's first play() adds its own start latency on top of the timer. This is throttled to one echo per 66 ms (game.js:57929), so it is a slapback rather than a wash - a real but secondary reinforcement of the "delayed" impression, on top of the launch-sample finding above. It correctly resets to 0 on gate miss (game.js:34911) and on s5RunReset (game.js:34800), so it does not leak into ordinary stage-5 combat before the run arms.
- FIX: Only if the launch-sample re-cut does not fully satisfy Mike. Minimal change: in A.play (game.js:57961) make the reflection opt-out for foreground weapon cues - guard the `A._warpSfx(...)` call with a check on the TAME entry, e.g. skip it when `cfg && cfg.native`, which already flags exactly the player-critical foreground bank (spaceShadowHit, spaceShadowRelease, spaceLaserCannon, spaceVolley*) at game.js:57849-57856 while leaving the warp reflection on ambience and explosions. Do not delete _warpSfx - it is what gives the warp its reverb in a build that cannot route media elements through a ConvolverNode (see the comment at game.js:57908-57910).
- risk: The warp sequence loses part of its reverb character on player weapon cues specifically, which may make the eight-gate run feel drier. It is guarded by cfg existing, so any sound without a TAME entry keeps current behaviour. Verify by ear during the armed gate run, not from source - per the project's own history these audio paths have twice been misdiagnosed from reading alone.

## Freezer fire/ice orb: remove the hold-to-charge mechanic and rename every player-facing string to THERMASHOCK

### [UNVERIFIED] The fire/ice orb is a hold-to-charge weapon. While Freezer's TIME FREEZE special is active, holding FIRE winds up a 1.5s charge instead of firing; a hold shorter than 0.675s fires nothing at all.
- where: game.js:23010
- now: chargePilotActive() (defined line 23002) is the single ownership gate that decides whether a held trigger charges or reaches the cadence block. Its Freezer clause is `if(k==='freezer' && run && run.weapon===5 && typeof orbIsFire==='function' && orbIsFire()) return true;`. orbIsFire() (line 25009) is true for BOTH 'fireorb' and 'fireice', and weaponVariant (lines 5482-5490) never gives Freezer 'fireorb' at any stage — so for Freezer, orbIsFire() true means EXACTLY the fireice orb. Returning true here sets _chgPilot at line 26032, which makes the branch at line 26055 (`else if(_chgPilot && firing) { /* winding up — the charge tick owns the trigger */ }`) swallow the trigger so pShoot() is never reached. Note the charge only exists while `special` is truthy (chargePilotActive returns false with no special, and freezerOrbCharge is only called from updateSpecial line 22592) — outside the special the orb already fires immediately.
- FIX: In chargePilotActive(), line 23010: add `&& !(typeof orbIsFireIce==='function' && orbIsFireIce())` to the freezer clause. That is the whole trigger-ownership change — with it false, the existing branch at line 26056 (`else if(firing && player.fireCd<=0)`) runs pShoot() on the normal cadence and the orb fires immediately.
- risk: chargePilotActive also drives the tap-window logic at lines 26032-26049 and gates spaceShadowTick (line 21296). Turning it off for Freezer means his fireice orb now fires on _weaponCadence() rather than SPECIAL_TAP_CD, so rate-of-fire during his special goes up; the pShoot w===5 branch caps live orbs at 1 (2 at lv5) via lines 21723-21725, which is the natural throttle.

### [UNVERIFIED] Even with the trigger gate changed, freezerOrbCharge() still runs every frame during the special: it builds _frzOrb.ch, plays the charge-start sting and the charge loop, and launches a SECOND projectile (a kind:'fireball') on release — so you would get both the cadence orb and a charged fireball.
- where: game.js:25032
- now: freezerOrbCharge(dt) (defined line 25026, called only from updateSpecial line 22592) bails on `if(!_mayCharge || !orbIsFire() || run.weapon!==5)` at line 25032. orbIsFire() is true for fireice, so it does NOT bail. It then: creates the _frzOrb state (line 25037), plays Audio.SFX.fireIceChargeStart on the first held frame (lines 25040-25044), ramps _frzOrb.ch toward FRZ_ORB_FULL = 1.5s (line 25022, accumulated line 25046), starts the sustained loop Snd.loopOn('fireIceChargeLoop') (lines 25048-25051), and on release calls `if(c>=FRZ_ORB_FULL*0.45) launchFireball(c)` (line 25055) — the 0.675s dead zone where a short hold produces nothing. The charge visual is freezerOrbDraw() (line 25129, called at line 54907), which draws the nts_chg_0..3 reel scaled by _frzOrb.ch/FRZ_ORB_FULL.
- FIX: In freezerOrbCharge(), line 25032: add `|| (typeof orbIsFireIce==='function' && orbIsFireIce())` to the bail condition. It already does the correct teardown on that path (loopOff at 25033, `_frzOrb=null` at 25034), and freezerOrbDraw() early-returns on `!_frzOrb` (line 25130), so the charge state, both charge sounds and the charge art all go away with that one clause. No other edit is needed for the mechanic — no other code creates _frzOrb.
- risk: launchFireball() (line 25073) is called from NOWHERE ELSE — line 25055 is its only call site. Disabling the fireice charge makes it unreachable, which retires the 3-tier speed/spin table (slow/half/full, lines 25077-25078), the 8-ray full-charge discharge (`_rays: full?8:0`, line 25123), and the authored nts_burst_* 8-point star drawn at lines 37084-37090 — the eight-way discharge Mike specified in drop 0801fj. If he wants that burst kept, it has to be moved onto the kind:'orb' push at lines 21734-21735 rather than left behind. The launch float text at line 25125 also becomes unreachable (see the next finding).

### [UNVERIFIED] After the charge is removed, the on-screen 'THERMOSHOCK!' name pop on launch disappears entirely — the orb fires silently as far as the name goes.

The uncharged path — the pShoot w===5 branch at lines 21722-21738 — pushes the kind:'orb' bullet and plays Audio.SFX.fireIceOrbLaunch (lines 21736-21738) but has no floatText of its own.

## stage6-blacksteel-raptor-plate

### [UNVERIFIED] The stage-6 miniboss jet is drawn nose-UP (pointing away from the player) instead of facing down the screen at the player, and its nose gun visibly fires out of its exhaust.

NOTHING IN CODE ROTATES OR FLIPS THE PLATE: shipBossDraw blits it at 17822 under shipBossVisualPose's P.rot only, which for this unit is (b._sbaBank||0)*0.055 (game.js:12966, ~+/-0.055 rad of bank), and the 0904k aerobatic scale-through-zero is switched off by RAP_AEROBATICS=false at game.js:15089. The reversal is baked into the source art. Measured with the project's own facing metric (_BUILD_SOURCE/audit_enemy_facing_0905.py: 'a craft facing SOUTH carries its bulk high and tapers to a nose at the BOTTOM, so MASS-Y sits BELOW 0.5; facing NORTH pushes it above 0.5'): all four raptor plates give MASS-Y 0.628-0.630 and taper (bottom-band ink / top-band ink) 4.33-4.35, against nsb_jungle_cruiser 0.477 / 0.72, nsb_siege_ember 0.496 / 0.98, nsb_void_bat 0.491 / 0.97, herald_intact 0.475 / 0.77. Mirror-LR IoU is 0.998 and the principal axis is 0.0 deg off vertical, so it is NOT turned, banked or 3/4-view - it is perfectly vertical and 180 degrees backwards. Corroboration from the mounts: SHIPBOSS.blacksteel declares mounts C:[0,0.43] and L/R:[-+0.25,0.12] (game.js:12256), and shipBossMount computes ly = b.h*M[1] with +y down the screen (game.js:12709), so the 'nose' mount C resolves to +146px BELOW centre - the tail of a north-facing plate - while every act fires at Math.PI/2 (straight down) from it (stage6MiniTick, game.js:15221/15238/15247). That audit script enumerates its roster from ENEMY_ART, which does not contain SHIPBOSS miniboss hulls, which is why the 0905 facing sweep never saw this unit.

Do not add a rotation in shipBossDraw - the header at game.js:17750 already forbids draw-time fixes to authored miniboss art, and a code flip would resample the plate and desync shipBossMount.
- risk: The mounts are fractions of the hull box, so flipping the art puts C (the nose gun) at the tail unless the mounts row at game.js:12256 is mirrored in y at the same time: C:[0,0.43] -> C:[0,-0.43] and L/R y 0.12 -> -0.12. Forgetting that trades a backwards jet for muzzle flashes detached from the hull. blacksteelHit's wing routing (game.js:15131) is x-only and is unaffected.

### [UNVERIFIED] The raptor is far too big - its hull fills roughly half the visible width and two thirds of the screen height, and its square hitbox extends ~48px of empty air past each wingtip.

The comment immediately above (game.js:12252-12254) records the 0904k doubling: 'Mike: "the raptor to be 2x as large ..." 170 -> 340.' shipBossInit copies the row verbatim - b.w=D.w; b.h=D.h (game.js:12587) - and shipBossDraw sizes the blit from it: const s=b.w/256, w=256*s, h=(b.h>0 ? b.h : 256*s) at game.js:17795, drawn at 17822. So the plate is drawn into a 340x340 box. There is NO _foot / ENEMY_ART_FOOT multiplier on this path: ENEMY_ART_FOOT (2.15, game.js:31664) and per-unit e._foot are read only by drawNewEnemyArt (game.js:31633) and the stage-9 helper (34738/34749), neither of which a _ship subBoss reaches - the SHIPBOSS w/h row is the only scale dial. Measured source: the 512x512 canvas carries ink at alpha bbox (72,10)-(439,502) = 367x492, identical on all four plates, so the drawn ink is 340*367/512 = 243.7px wide by 340*492/512 = 326.7px tall. Against VW=480 / VH=512 (game.js:14) with the stage-6 world 680 wide (assets/game/bg_stage06_master.png is 680x4080), that is 50.8% of the camera window's width and 63.8% of screen height. Every other miniboss in the same table is roughly half that: junglecruiser 168x168 (12282), thornrime 165x165 (12244), siegeember 165x165 (12029), olivewarden and spawncarrier 210x216 (12214/12235), heralddeath 150x200 (12240). Because the canvas is square and the box is square the aspect is correct - there is no squash to fix, only size. Separately, the collision paths read subBoss.w/subBoss.h (e.g. game.js:27363, 27431), i.e. a 340x340 square against 243.7x326.7 of ink, so ~48px each side of transparent canvas counts as hull; shrinking the row tightens that in the same edit.

A smaller hull is a harder target and the stage-6 fight gets measurably longer - that is a balance change Mike should see. The two fallback literals '340' hardcoded at game.js:15133 and 15203 become stale defaults; they only fire if b.w is missing, but they are worth updating in the same edit so a later reader is not misled.

Also re-run the black-edge pass (_BUILD_SOURCE/black_edge_bosses_0904o.py) after any regeneration, and re-measure the alpha bbox: shipBossDraw places the plate by its canvas, so a changed footprint moves the hull relative to its mounts and its 340-box hitbox.

## Stage 4 boss (STORM SOVEREIGN MK II) — shield impact cue, blue-lightning cadence, projectile scale, energy-section pixel glow, and attack telegraphs

### [UNVERIFIED] The sound a player shot makes when it hits the stage-4 boss's shield is wrong and grating. It is a generic laser-tick sample, retriggered at full volume on every single round with no throttle.
- where: game.js:13723
- now: stage4ShieldDeflectBullet() (assets/game.js:13700-13723) ends with `stage4WarfareSound('shieldHit','shieldHitLight')`. `shieldHit` DOES NOT EXIST anywhere in Audio.SFX — line 13723 is the only occurrence of that identifier in the whole file — so stage4WarfareSound (13754) always falls through to the fallback `shieldHitLight`. That fallback is assets/game/sounds/shield_hit_light.wav (registered at 57680), which is ALSO bound to `laserBeamHit` (57630) and `spaceLaserHit` (57645): it is the engine's generic laser-hit tick, not a shield cue. Two aggravating factors: (a) `shieldHitLight` has no entry in the Snd.TAME table (the table runs 57806-57879), and A.play (57933) only applies a retrigger gate when `TAME[name].min` exists — so it fires unshaped and unthrottled once per deflected bullet, whereas the sibling path stage4ShieldAbsorbHit (13696) DOES gate itself behind `H.sfx`. (b) purpose-built shield cues exist and are unused here: `shieldImpact` / `shieldImpactHeavy` (6293 / 6297 — authored in drop 0904q specifically as "the impact cue, distorted and pitch-shifted" at Mike's request), and `shieldDeflect` / `bossShieldStatic` (6369 / 6368), which the comment at 6361 literally labels the "STORM SOVEREIGN SHIELD KIT". `shieldHitHeavy` (57681) and `projectileRicochet` (57684) are the other shield-ish samples available.
- FIX: In stage4ShieldDeflectBullet (assets/game.js:13723) replace `stage4WarfareSound('shieldHit','shieldHitLight')` with a gated call that names a cue that actually resolves and reuses the shield's existing sfx cooldown field: `if(H.sfx<=0){H.sfx=.06;stage4WarfareSound('shieldImpact','shieldDeflect');}` (H is already in scope from line 13701). Use 'shieldDeflect' as primary instead if Mike prefers the sharp electric snap over the heavy absorbed thud. No other line changes.
- risk: H.sfx is shared with stage4ShieldAbsorbHit (13690/13696), so gating the deflect cue on it also slightly rate-limits the node-hit cue; use a separate field (e.g. H.dsfx) if that matters. shieldImpact is a synth cue (toneDist at 6177) rather than a sample, so it is quieter than the .wav it replaces and may need a level pass.

### [UNVERIFIED] The blue lightning attack fires rarely — and it can never fire while the shield is up, which is most of the fight.
- where: game.js:13971
- now: The attack is the `'lightning'` MODE of the boss's own state machine (the terminal `else` branch of stage4WarfareBossTick, 13973-13990), NOT the SHIPBOSS pattern named `s4lightning` (declared in `pats` at 11984, slots at 12805, profile at 12845) — that pattern is dead for this boss, see the telegraph finding. The mode machine is a fixed loop with no randomisation: shield -> (all four nodes destroyed, 13655-13657) -> burst (exits at S.t>=4.25, 13958) -> flyaway (exits at S.t>=3.55, 13966) -> swerve (exits at S.t>=1.72, 13971) -> lightning (exits at S.t>=4.12, 13990) -> burst. So lightning is reached once per ~13.6 s of scaled mode time, and inside it only three bolt volleys fire (13975 wings, 13979 wings, 13983 centre lance) plus one orb (13987). Shield state gates it hard: the 'shield' branch (13934-13946) only ever hands off to 'burst', and only when `!H.active`; the shield re-arms at 75/50/25% HP (thresholds at 13395, gate at 13682-13686) which forces 'rearm' -> 'shield' again (13749-13750). Net effect: zero lightning while the shield is up, one short lightning window per 13.6 s while it is down — the opposite of what Mike is asking for.
- FIX: One-line cadence change in stage4WarfareBossTick: at assets/game.js:13958 make the burst exit alternate into lightning while the shield is down — `if(S.t>=4.25)stage4WarfareSetMode(b,(S.shield&&!S.shield.active&&!S.shield.rearming&&(S.round&1))?'lightning':'flyaway');`. That roughly doubles lightning frequency only in the shield-down state and leaves the shield-up rhythm untouched. If more is wanted, additionally shorten the lightning branch's event gaps at 13974/13978/13982 (1.12/1.68/2.28) rather than adding new events.
- risk: Skipping 'flyaway' on alternate rounds also skips the two shootable orbs spawned at 13964 and the swerve reposition at 13968-13971, so the boss will hold centre more and the fight loses some orb pressure; `S.round` is incremented in stage4WarfareSetMode (13757) so the alternation is stable but shared with every other mode change.

### [UNVERIFIED] The projectiles the stage-4 boss itself fires read as small; the drawn size levers are per-call and the boss's own rounds sit BELOW 1.0 on every one of them.
- where: game.js:13787
- now: stage4WarfareShot (13788-13800) computes two independent sizes. HITBOX = the per-role base `_sz` at 13791 (orb [28,28], lightning [13,30], spread [10,22], everything else [7,18]) times `S4H_SHOT_HIT`=1.18 (13787, applied 13792). DRAWN size = the per-role art height in drawStage4WarfareProjectile (33489-33493: mg 28, lightningmg 34, spread 38, orb 62, lance 54; and the 'lightning' bolt goes through combatAtlasDraw at 62x108, 33486) times `b.szMul`, which is set at 13794 as `(opt.szMul||1)*S4H_SHOT_DRAW` with S4H_SHOT_DRAW=1.45. The carrier's OWN shots all pass szMul BELOW 1: shield-mode orbs .76 (13943), burst chaingun rounds .78 (13953), flyaway orbs .80 (13964), centre orb .92 (13987) — so after the 1.45 they land at 1.10/1.13/1.16/1.33 effective, i.e. a burst round draws ~38 px tall and a shield orb ~68 px. Only the three lightning volleys are already large (1.18/1.22/1.68 -> 1.71/1.77/2.44). Note also that the centre lance at 13983 passes explicit `w:23,h:54`, which takes the `opt.w||size[0]` branch at 13793 and therefore BYPASSES the 1.18 hitbox multiplier entirely. The 1.45/1.18 constants are documented (comment 13774-13786) as being for the HELPER turrets, but stage4WarfareShot is the single funnel for the carrier, the helpers (13580), the wall (13809) and the Olive Warden too.
- FIX: Raise the per-call `szMul` values for the carrier's own weapons rather than the shared constants: assets/game.js:13943 (.76), 13953 (.78), 13964 (.80), 13987 (.92) — e.g. multiply each by ~1.4. Leave `S4H_SHOT_DRAW` at 13787 alone, because it also scales the Olive Warden and the helper chainguns which Mike already signed off on in drop 0905c. If the hitbox should grow with the art, bump the per-role base at 13791 instead of S4H_SHOT_HIT so only the carrier's roles move.
- risk: Drawn size and hitbox are deliberately decoupled (comment 13778-13785); enlarging only the draw widens the gap between what the player sees and what kills them. Enlarging the orbs also enlarges the shootable orbs (`shootable:true`, 13943/13964/13987), making them easier to snipe — a small difficulty change in the player's favour.

### [UNVERIFIED] The pixel-glow pass over the boss's energy sections never appears — for the stage-4 boss or for any boss in the game.
- where: game.js:32674
- now: The feature Mike is asking for is already written: drawBossEnergyGlow (24206-24232), whose header comment at 24191-24204 quotes him verbatim ("these guys need to get pixel glow effects on their energy sections"). It extracts the saturated/bright pixels of the currently drawn plate via xartEmissive (7676-7705) and re-burns them additively on two clocks. It is UNREACHABLE. In drawBoss (32668-32685): line 32672 sets `_bossFade = (_b && _b.dead) ? bossDeathAlpha(_b.dying||0) : 1`; bossDeathAlpha (28744-28747) returns literally `T < BOSS_FADE_HOLD ? 1 : 0` — only 1 or 0, and the comment at 32669 says so explicitly ("deliberately binary ... never a dissolve"). Line 32673 returns when `_bossFade<=0`; line 32674 draws and RETURNS when `_bossFade>=1`. The two calls to drawBossEnergyGlow / drawBossEnergyField sit at 32678-32679, inside the only branch that requires 0 < _bossFade < 1 — a value bossDeathAlpha can never produce. The stage-4 boss would otherwise be an ideal subject: its default `_animKey` is the `s4w_boss_energized_*` reel (13995), all 12 frames are present on disk, and drawBossEnergyGlow keys off `b._animKey` at 24209-24210.
- FIX: In drawBoss (assets/game.js), hoist the two calls out of the dead branch: change line 32674 from `if(_bossFade >= 1){drawBossInner();encounterDamageOverlay(_b,false);return;}` to also run the glow — `if(_bossFade >= 1){drawBossInner();if(typeof drawBossEnergyGlow==='function'&&boss)drawBossEnergyGlow(boss);if(typeof drawBossEnergyField==='function'&&boss)drawBossEnergyField(boss);encounterDamageOverlay(_b,false);return;}`. Leave 32678-32679 as they are so the dying-fade path is unchanged.
- risk: This turns the glow on for EVERY boss at once, not just stage 4 — stages 1-9 will all change appearance, which is the drop's stated intent but is a broad visual change that should be eyeballed per stage. xartEmissive rasterises and caches one canvas per plate key (7676-7705); an animated boss cycles 12 frames per reel, so expect ~12-36 cached canvases per boss on first sight, and a one-frame hitch the first time each new frame is glowed. It is already guarded against tainted-canvas throws (7688-7692), which on a file:// build silently yields no glow rather than an error.

### [UNVERIFIED] The stage-4 boss gives no wind-up or warning before it attacks — no anticipation pose, no pre-flash, no charge cue for most of its weapons.
- where: game.js:13927
- now: The engine's ship-boss tell layer never engages for this boss. shipBossQueueAttack (12904-12933) is what creates `b._sba` — the tell/recover/kick record read by shipBossVisualPose (12962-12976) for the anticipation pulse, and the thing that fires the pre-volley muzzle flash (12928) and `bossWeaponCharge` (12931). It is called only from 29254 and 18639, both gated on `b.fireCd<=0`. stage4WarfareBossTick sets `b.fireCd=999` on EVERY frame (assets/game.js:13927, plus once at init 13419), and shipBossManoeuvre returns early through stage4WarfareTick at 15265, so the cooldown never reaches zero and `b._sba` is never created. That also makes the profile row `s4lightning:{tell:0.64,recover:0.32,kick:11,shake:5}` (12845) and the slot list at 12805 dead for this boss. What the boss DOES have today: entering 'lightning' mode plays bossWeaponCharge (13761) and runs the `s4w_boss_charge_*` reel for its first 1.18 s (13993) ahead of the first bolt at S.t>=1.12 (13974); the helper turrets play bossWeaponCharge once per windup (13563). Everything else — burst chaingun (13950-13957), shield orbs (13938-13945), flyaway orbs (13962-13965) — releases cold. For comparison, other bosses in this file DO telegraph: carrierThunderheadStart/Tick/Draw (17133-17175) holds a `tell:.66` window, paints the threatened lightning lanes and the safe gap while `T.t<T.tell`, calls floatText with the attack's name and plays bossPhase; and the Stage 2/3 laser telegraph l23WarnSymbolDraw / l23WarnSound (14374-14418) blinks the authored `nwarn_yield` -> `nwarn_alert` triangular sign above the hull over L23_WARN_T=3.0 s (14370) with one alert beep per revealed arrow.
- FIX: Add a tell window to the mode machine rather than reviving _sba: in stage4WarfareSetMode (assets/game.js:13758) set `S.tell=(mode==='burst'?.45:mode==='flyaway'?.40:0)` alongside the existing resets, delay each branch's first shot by that amount (the `S.shot=-.02` seed at 13758 becomes `S.shot=S.tell`), and in stage4WarfareDrawOver (14010) draw `nwarn_yield`/`nwarn_alert` above the hull while `S.t<S.tell`, copying the blink/flip logic of l23WarnSymbolDraw (14374-14392) including its `L23_WARN_MINY`=46 floor so the sign clears the boss health gauge.
- risk: Delaying the first shot of each mode by 0.4-0.45 s lowers the boss's DPS slightly; the modes exit on fixed S.t thresholds (13958/13966/13971/13990) so a tell eats into that mode's firing time rather than lengthening the cycle. The warning sign must be clamped like l23WarnSymbolDraw or it draws off-screen or under the boss gauge — the comment at 14382-14395 records both of those failures happening before.

### [UNVERIFIED] The engine's generic boss telegraph ring is inert: nothing can ever set it, and its function has been silently overwritten.
- where: game.js:29347
- now: Two global `function bossTelegraph` declarations exist in the same scope: the 5-arg ring setter `bossTelegraph(b,x,y,dur,col)` at assets/game.js:29347, which assigns `b._tel`, and the mech-boss draw routine `bossTelegraph(b,K)` at 47212. Function declarations hoist and the later one wins, so the 5-arg version is unreachable by name for the entire program. It also has zero callers — the only references to the identifier in the file are the two definitions (29347, 47212) and the single call `bossTelegraph(b,K)` at 48075. Consequently `b._tel` is never assigned, and bossTelegraphDraw (29351-29363), which is called every frame from drawBossInner at 32689, returns immediately at 29354 (`if(!b || !b._tel) return`) for every boss in the game. Any attempt to add a telegraph to the stage-4 boss by calling bossTelegraph(boss,x,y,dur,col) would hit the mech version, evaluate `K.fight` on a number, and silently no-op.
- FIX: Rename the ring setter at assets/game.js:29347 to a unique name (e.g. `bossTelegraphRing`) so it stops being shadowed by the mech-boss draw at 47212, then call that name from wherever a telegraph is wanted. bossTelegraphDraw (29351) needs no change — it reads `b._tel` and will start working as soon as something can set it.
- risk: Low in isolation, since nothing calls the 5-arg form today. Verify with a grep that no non-obvious dynamic lookup (e.g. `window['bossTelegraph']`) depends on the current shadowing before renaming.

## Lizzie's heavy machine-gun mount (lzMount / "HEAVY MG") — fire cadence

### [CONFIRMED] Mike wants Lizzie's machine-gun attachment to fire 10% slower. The attachment is the HEAVY MG vehicle mount (lzMount) that arcs in, shrinks, docks at her nose and fires twin-barrel heavy slugs in place of her primary for 15 seconds. Its whole fire cadence lives in one constant.
- where: game.js:23653
- now: VERIFIED LINE BY LINE. assets/game.js:23653 reads exactly `const LZ_SLUG_CD  = 0.06875;`. assets/game.js:20138 is exactly `if(typeof lzMountActive==='function' && lzMountActive()) return LZ_SLUG_CD;` and it sits before every other branch of the cadence table (the WEAPON_CADENCE lookup and the per-weapon level ramp both come after it, at 20147+), so a docked mount short-circuits the table exactly as claimed. lzMountActive() at assets/game.js:23662 is `!!(lzMount && lzMount.docked && (typeof _pilotKey!=='function' || _pilotKey()==='lizzie'))`. The caller at assets/game.js:26062 is real, though the report quoted it abridged; the full line is `player.fireCd=(cd!=null?cd:0.2)*(specialActive('maverick')||specialActive('yuri')?1:(1-(PILOTMOD?PILOTMOD.fire:0))); pShoot();` — the maverick/yuri bypass never applies while the pilot is Lizzie, so the multiplicative PILOTMOD.fire path the report describes is the one that runs. Lizzie's stat card at assets/game.js:7126 is `{key:'lizzie', name:'LIZZIE', role:'BOMBSHELL', tint:'#ffc21a', font:4, spd:-0.02,fire:0.08, range:0.16}` and PILOTMOD is populated from it at assets/game.js:24460, so the effective interval is 0.06875 x 0.92 = 0.063250s (~15.81 volleys/sec). I checked the one path that could have broken that arithmetic: the tap-fire branch at assets/game.js:26045-26046 sets fireCd from `_weaponCadence()` WITHOUT the PILOTMOD multiplier, but it is gated by `if(_chgPilot)` at 26032-26033 (chargePilotActive() — Maverick/Falva/Cole), so Lizzie never reaches it. The slug push site in pShoot() at assets/game.js:21582 carries no cooldown of its own, and the comment directly above at 21578-21579 confirms why: 'The former fixed-1/60 second cooldown only advanced when pShoot ran, stretching 75ms into roughly a quarter-second.' The 0831 house precedent is real: assets/game.js:23651-23652 reads '+25% between twin-barrel beats', and 0.055 x 1.25 = 0.06875 exactly, confirming the convention is applied to the INTERVAL. 23653 is the only owner of this trigger.
- FIX: At assets/game.js:23653 change `const LZ_SLUG_CD  = 0.06875;` to `const LZ_SLUG_CD  = 0.075625;` (0.06875 x 1.10). Effective cadence 0.063250s -> 0.069575s, 15.81 -> 14.37 volleys/sec. Change nothing else: leave LZ_SLUG_DMG (7, confirmed at assets/game.js:23638), LZ_SLUG_SPD (7.875, confirmed at assets/game.js:23654) and LZ_LIFE (15, confirmed at assets/game.js:23658) alone — the comment at 23639-23650 records that damage was measured for the one-or-two-shot brief and is explicitly not the lever ('The DAMAGE is untouched at 7'). Append a short note to the 23651 comment recording the -10% pass, matching local style. Interpretation caveat stands: if Mike means 'rate x 0.9' the value is 0.0763889, but the 0831 precedent at 23651 favours the interval reading.
- risk: Low gameplay risk; one QA check fails unless updated with it (see next entry). Directionally safe — the 0810f comment at assets/game.js:23639-23641 quotes Mike calling 0.16 'way too slow', and 0.075625 is less than half that and still well under the ~0.20s primary it replaces, so it does not regress into the complaint. DPS over the 15s window drops ~9% (221 -> 201 dmg/sec at 7 dmg x 2 barrels), which may nudge stage-8 fodder time-to-kill; the shots-to-kill table in the 23626-23637 comment is a function of LZ_SLUG_DMG only and is unaffected. Bullet kind 'lzslug' is untouched, so collision (assets/game.js:7480, 37788), art and audio paths are not involved. Note the second-order effect the report did not raise: LZ_SLUG_CD is the value _weaponCadence() returns, and player.fireCd also gates the auto-missile fan indirectly only via its own run._mslCd, so missiles are unaffected — nothing else consumes this constant.

## Stage 4 boss (Storm Sovereign, kind 'stormsovereign') — the two "helper" chaingun turrets S.coreTurrets: spin, round size, and world/camera anchoring

### [UNVERIFIED] The helpers' chainguns never appear to spin. Mike: "chainguns on helpers need to rotate 360 degrees to appear spinning."
- where: game.js:14074
- now: The helper body is drawn once per turret in stage4WarfareDrawOver. The only rotation applied is aim, not roll: line 14073 does ctx.rotate((t.ang==null?PI/2:t.ang)-PI/2), and t.ang is only ever PI/2 or PI/2 +/- PI/4 (set at 13595-13596, eased at 13557). All the "spin" is a frame index: line 13598 advances t.spin=(t.spin+dt*t.reelSpeed)%8 and line 14062 picks frame=(Math.floor(t.spin)||0)%8 into dual='s4w_helper_dual_'+frame (14063), drawn at 14074. Those 8 frames CANNOT show rotation: _BUILD_SOURCE/stage4_warfare/build_stage4_warfare_assets.py:462-476 bakes them in save_locked_dual_helper_assets(), compositing the gun into both sockets at a hard-coded (math.pi/2, math.pi/2) (line 473-474), and its own docstring says "neither gun nor the plate rotates or gimbals". I measured the shipped frames: alpha silhouette is identical across all 8 (6160-6256 opaque px, centroid stable to 0.8px) and RGB mean difference frame 00 vs 04 is 7.4/255 - a cyan glow pulse from energized_frame(), nothing more. So at reelSpeed 42-51 the turret is flickering a glow at ~6 cycles/sec, which is exactly "does not look like it is spinning". Note the real barrel art already ships and is already registered but is drawn NOWHERE in the engine: s4w_final_chaingun_0..7 (128x192, built from storm_sovereign_chaingun_fullrotate_generated.png) and s4w_final_chaingun_core_0..7 (224x224), registered at game.js:2181-2182 and only touched by the warm-up list at 12615.
- FIX: Separate barrel ROLL from aim ANGLE, and assemble the helper at runtime from the parts the bake already produced. (1) In stage4CoreTurretTick, beside line 13598, add a free-running roll: t.roll=((t.roll||0)+dt*t.reelSpeed*0.55)%TAU; (initialise t.roll:0 in the turret literal at 13480-13484). (2) In stage4WarfareDrawOver, replace the single drawImage of `dual` at line 14074 with the three-part assembly using the builder's own geometry (composite_socketed_assembly, py:623-630: canvas 160, centre (80,70), core_size 96, gun_size (44,68), socket offset 92/224*96 = 39.43). With k=S4H_SIZE/160 (=1.2375): draw s4w_final_chaingun_<frame> twice at (+/-39.43*k, -10*k) relative to the turret centre, each wrapped in its own save/rotate(t.roll)/restore and sized 44*k x 68*k, then draw s4w_final_chaingun_core_<frame> centred at (0,-10*k) at 96*k. Leave line 14073's aim rotate, stage4CoreTurretTip (13538-13543) and every hitbox untouched, so aim, muzzle flashes and rounds keep leaving the barrels exactly as they do today.
- risk: The heat overlay (14075-14078) and both xartTint flashes (14079-14081) key off the baked 'dual'/'s4w_helper_dual_hot_' plate, so after the swap they will ghost a STATIC pair of barrels over the spinning ones - those three passes must be rebuilt from the same parts (or tinted per-part via xartTint on the s4w_final_chaingun_* keys). Also, every one of the new drawImages needs its own XART.rdy() guard: if you gate on rdy(dual) and draw the parts, or gate the parts and skip the core, the helper renders as a partial silhouette. The cheaper-looking alternative - just adding +t.roll to line 14073's rotate - is wrong: t.ang alone feeds stage4CoreTurretTip, so rounds and muzzle flashes would spawn off a barrel that is no longer pointing there.

DRAW: line 13580 fires with szMul:.80, and stage4WarfareShot passes szMul:(opt.szMul||1)*S4H_SHOT_DRAW (13794, S4H_SHOT_DRAW=1.45 at 13787), so the net draw multiplier is 1.16, not 1.45. drawStage4WarfareProjectile gives role 'lightningmg' a base h=34 (33490) times mul, so the round paints ~39px tall / ~24px wide - only 16% over base, and the 0905c "the helpers' rounds are bigger" note at 13773-13786 is being cancelled at this one call site. ### [UNVERIFIED] The helpers slide sideways with the screen instead of holding their position. Mike: "helpers should not scroll with the screen, they remain where they are."
- where: game.js:13470

Stage 4's world is 680 wide (_levelCfg case 4, line 3407: plateW:680) and VIEW_FIT is 0 (line 1253) so viewW()=VW=480 - camX eases across 0..200 following the player (updateCamX, 1422-1427). The turrets are drawn in world space under drawWorld's translate(-camX) (call sites 17746/17835), so as the player crosses the level the two helpers travel up to 200px sideways in the world, riding the screen. The y half of the same line is a second follow: cy is b._drawY, so they also chase the carrier's hull vertically (including through its charge/ram manoeuvres). The 13467-13469 comment records this as deliberate ("they hold the two outer combat lanes while the carrier strafes") - it is the decision Mike is now reversing.

In stage4CoreTurretSpawnMissing (13472-13489), after const p=stage4CoreTurretTarget(b,side) at 13478, store it on the turret literal: homeX:p.x, homeY:p.y. Then in stage4CoreTurretTick change line 13553's const p=stage4CoreTurretTarget(b,t.side) to const p=(t.homeX!=null)?{x:t.homeX,y:t.homeY}:stage4CoreTurretTarget(b,t.side); - the existing lerp at 13556 then eases each helper onto its fixed spot and holds it there. Leave stage4CoreTurretTarget itself as the spawn-time placement. For a placement that cannot end up half off-world, seed homeX from the world instead of the camera: worldWidth()*.5+side*168.
- risk: This is the point of the change, but name it: a world-latched helper can leave the 480px window (camX 0..200 over a 680 world), so at one extreme of the level a helper is off-screen and cannot be shot - or shoot the player fairly. Its hit tests (stage4CoreTurretAt 13491, BeamAt 13497, FlameAt 13505) all read t.x/t.y so they stay correct, but stage4CoreSpreadOffsets (13603-13611) assumes both helpers are in the outer lanes when it narrows the carrier's fan to [-.14,0,.14]; with a helper parked off-screen that fan is narrow for no visible reason. Also, since spawn happens at the 50% threshold (13669) while the camera is wherever the player left it, two runs of the fight will place the helpers differently - use the worldWidth() seed if a repeatable arena matters.

## stage5-scroll-halts-and-space-transition

### [UNVERIFIED] Stage 5's entry decelerates to a literal dead stop right before the ship converts. Mike: "DO NOT STOP SCROLLING THE level or speed."
- where: game.js:54195
- now: drawLaunch's phase machine sends every stage except 6 from 'run' into 'brake' (line 54186: `if(_s6){launchBeginCountdown();} else {drawLaunch._phase='brake';...}`). The brake body at 54195 is `drawLaunch._spd=lerp(1750,0,_ease(p))` over a full 2.0 s — it ends at ZERO. Worse for stage 5 specifically: `_spdCap` is 3200 for space stages (54179) and the run phase accelerates at 2600px/s (54181), so `_spd` is at 3200 when brake begins and the first brake frame snaps it to 1750 — a visible slam — then decays to 0. `drawLaunch._bgScroll` (54249) floors at `Math.max(LAUNCH_COUNTDOWN_SCROLL, _spd)` = 24, so the background goes 3200 -> 24 px/s in two seconds. Stage 6 was explicitly exempted from this exact beat (comment at 54188-54189 and 54226-54232, drop 0903x, Mike: "with stage 6, NO SLOWING DOWN IN THE TRANSITION"); stage 5 never got the same exemption even though it is the other high-speed space stage.
- FIX: In drawLaunch (assets/game.js), floor the brake at a cruise speed for the gravity stage instead of zero: at line 54195 change `drawLaunch._spd=lerp(1750,0,_ease(p))` to `drawLaunch._spd=lerp(_gravityStage?_spdCap:1750, _gravityStage?STAGE5_SPACE_CRUISE:0, _ease(p))`, adding `const STAGE5_SPACE_CRUISE=260;` beside LAUNCH_COUNTDOWN_SCROLL at line 54063 (same value stage 6 already uses). Do not delete the brake phase — the ship-pose settle at 54313 and launchConnDy's 'brake' case at 54058 both key off it.
- risk: launchConnDy() returns the raw join distance during 'run'/'brake' (54058) and only eases it to 0 during 'settle' (54060). Keeping brake but ending it at speed is safe because _dist already exceeds LAND (SEG_B3+1400=12400) by the end of 'run' for stage 5 (_launchEnd = SEG_B3+SEG_B1+240 = 13240, line 54183), so the connector is already seated at dy 0. Skipping brake outright would drop the brake SFX cue and the banked-thruster pose.

### [UNVERIFIED] The entire ship-becomes-a-spaceship beat plays out at a 24 px/s crawl — roughly 15 seconds of near-frozen background — which is what reads as "the scroll stopped".
- where: game.js:54199

No structural change — the same shape as the stage-6 exemption already in those two lines.

Fix this finding together with the loopMaster/clamp findings below, or clamp the launch-side advance to the cruise the band needs (~120 px/s crosses the 512px screen in 4.3 s) rather than the full 260.

### [UNVERIFIED] The space background never actually "tiles up" over the stage — it arrives as a cross-fade instead, which is the thing an earlier drop explicitly ruled out.
- where: game.js:54107
- now: stage5SkyToSpaceDraw builds space as a clipped band whose leading edge is `yb=clamp(scroll-_at,0,VH)` (54107), with `_at` latched the first frame `stage5SpaceAscentProgress()` goes positive (54105-54106). That function returns 0 for the whole 'drift' phase and only goes positive at the start of 'charge' (54081-54093), so the band starts sweeping deep inside the gravity beat — where `scroll` (drawLaunch._bgScroll, 54249) is advancing at 24 px/s. Crossing VH=512 takes 512/24 = 21.3 s, but only ~7.8 s of gravity remain, so the band gets ~188px down a 512px screen and the sweep never completes. Line 54119 then paints the real level over the top with `globalAlpha=clamp((p-.72)/.28,0,1)` — a cross-fade, which is precisely what the block's own comment at 54096-54099 says Mike ruled out ("theres no fading anymore with the stage 5 transition"). Separately the band tiles the plate at `scroll*0.42` (54113) while the level it hands to runs at 1.0, so even the pixels that do move move at 42% rate.
- FIX: No change needed here if the two speed findings above are fixed — at a cruise of ~120-260 px/s the band crosses the screen in 2-4 s and completes well before p reaches .72, so the alpha ramp at 54119 becomes a no-op tail. If the sweep still needs to lead the fusion flash, latch `_at` earlier by making stage5SpaceAscentProgress return a small positive value during 'drift' (line 54085 `if(p==='drift')return 0;` -> return a ramp on gravityMode.dialogueT) rather than by touching the band math.
- risk: Latching _at during 'drift' starts space arriving before the kit does, which the comment at 54082-54084 deliberately prevents ("The horizon does not disappear before the kit arrives"). Prefer fixing the speed and leaving the ascent curve alone. Also verify the 0.42 tile rate at 54113 against gameplay's 1.0 rate or the stars will change speed at the handoff.

### [UNVERIFIED] During play, stage 5's starfield stops dead at the miniboss and again at the boss — the whole space background freezes while the fight happens.
- where: game.js:4742
- now: drawLevelMaster pins mapScroll for any boss or miniboss: `_bossHold` ramps 0->1 over ~0.6 s and line 4539 makes it `mapScroll = Math.min(range, mapScroll + dt*40*(1-_bossHold))` — 40 px/s to 0. For the real boss it is worse: stage 5's cfg (line 3425) declares no `continuousBoss`, so `_realBossRun` takes the arena branch at line 4655 and returns at 4709 with `_loopDraw(arenaImg, mapScroll)` — also frozen, and never reaching the loopMaster branch at all.
