- risk: Adding continuousBoss:true to case 5 also disables the arena path for stage 5 — harmless here because cfg.arena and cfg.arenaLiquid are both absent, so line 4709 was already drawing the same plate with the same _loopDraw mapping. Combat pacing is unaffected: mapScroll still holds, and the miniboss gate at 26233 (`mapScroll>=SB.afterScroll`) plus the boss triggers still read mapScroll, not the new visual clock — the same separation stage 6's comment at 4744-4745 calls out ("combat continues to read mapScroll").

### [UNVERIFIED] Stage 5's scroll is hard-clamped to the plate's pixel height even though its master is declared as a looping tile, so the background can stop permanently mid-stage.
- where: game.js:4543
- now: `mapScroll = Math.min(range, mapScroll + dt*40)` (4543, and the boss arm at 4539) where `range` comes from the block at 4487-4493: `cfg.scrollLen` if present, else `img.naturalHeight - VH`. Stage 5's cfg (line 3425) sets `loopMaster:true` but declares NO `scrollLen`, and bg_stage05_loop.png measures 680x3621, so range = 3621-512 = 3109 px. A looping plate has no end, so the clamp is meaningless as a limit but absolute as a stop: once mapScroll reaches 3109 the space background halts forever. Stage 5's authored length is 56 s (STAGES entry, line 8103) = 2240 px at 40 px/s, so normal play stays under — but the launch phases advance mapScroll before PLAY (54201, 54219, 54235, 54244), the rift/portal advances it at 230 px/s (line 16155), and the resume path restores a saved scroll (25420-25421), all of which eat into the margin. This is the identical failure the stage-6 comment at 3441-3447 records: "the level stopped dead a fifth of the way in, which is exactly the 'stopped scrolling, couldn't see or attack' Mike hit", fixed there by declaring scrollLen.
- FIX: Add an explicit length to the stage-5 config at line 3425, e.g. `scrollLen:4000` (56 s x 40 px/s plus headroom), so `range` states the stage's length rather than inheriting the plate's pixel height. Alternatively skip the clamp for looping masters: at 4543 use `mapScroll = cfg.loopMaster ? mapScroll + dt*40 : Math.min(range, mapScroll + dt*40)`.
- risk: `range` is also the denominator for `scrollFrac` (line 4763) and for stagePos()/levelScrollRange() (310-317), which gate scenery, road signs and the 0..1 progress readout. Changing it shifts where scroll-gated content lands. The scrollLen route is the safer of the two because it is the mechanism stage 6 already uses and every consumer already understands it; the loopMaster-skip route changes clamping semantics for stage 6 as well.

### [UNVERIFIED] On a cold cache the "space" that sweeps in during the stage-5 transition is a flat starless black rectangle (or nothing at all on the outbound leg) instead of the tiled space plate.
- where: game.js:25243
- now: Classic rdy()-gate-with-a-different-fallback, caused upstream by a warm that targets the wrong stage. beginStage calls `warmStage(arguments[0])` at line 25243, but `run.stage=num` is not set until line 25272 — and warmStage's `add(cfg.master)` at line 24705 reads `_levelCfg(n)`, which IGNORES its argument and switches on run.stage (definition at 3256; the trap is documented verbatim at line 819: "_levelCfg() IGNORES ITS ARGUMENT"). So entering stage 5 from stage 4 re-warms bg_stage04_master and never queues bg_stage05_loop. It is not picked up anywhere else either: its path is assets/game/bg_stage05_loop.png so the folder regex at 24698 (`levels/level5/|stages/5/`) misses it, and the stage-5 prefix list at 24773 does not name it (stage 6's list DOES name stage6_blue_master). Consequences: stageLoadReady(5) (24611) reports ready without the 5.3 MB master, so the 'load' gate at 54236 releases early; stage5SkyToSpaceDraw gates the tile on `XART.rdy('bg_stage05_loop')` at 54111 and its fallback at 54110 is `ctx.fillStyle='#020610'` — a plain near-black fill; and the outbound 4->5 leg's space band calls s45Tile at line 955, which returns false when not rdy (line 886) and draws nothing, leaving seqCfg(4).fill (outboundDraw line 1061) showing where space should be.
- FIX: Make beginStage warm the stage it is entering: move the `warmStage(arguments[0])` call at line 25243 to just after `run.stage=num` at line 25272, OR give _levelCfg a real parameter and have warmStage pass n. The minimal, lowest-blast-radius edit is the second half of the belt-and-braces: add 'bg_stage05_loop' to the stage-5 entry of the _F prefix table at line 24773, exactly as stage 6 lists 'stage6_blue_master'.
- risk: Moving the warmStage call changes what every stage preloads (today each stage warms its predecessor's master), so masters that currently arrive by luck could regress — verify stages 1-9 entry after the move. Adding the key to the _F[5] list at 24773 is inert by comparison but leaves the _levelCfg(n) argument bug in place for every other stage; the note at line 819 shows it has already been found once and left.

## Spread level 4 renders black because the shared WLV_BODY palette-swap table maps tier 4 to a 'black' multiply, and the spread draw path (unlike the machine gun pellet) has nothing in front of it to win first

### [CONFIRMED] The player SPREAD weapon (weapon index 1) at level 4 fires a near-black round instead of the white one Mike's tier scheme calls for (lv1 orange, lv2 blue, lv3 green, lv4 WHITE, lv5 red).
- where: game.js:36844
- now: Verified end to end in source, and the pixel claims re-measured independently. (1) game.js:36844 is exactly `const WLV_BODY={1:'#8a5a2b', 2:'#c8d2dc', 3:'white', 4:'black',` with `5:null, 6:'#3a8aff', 7:'#ff3b30', 8:null` on 36845 — tier 4's BODY is 'black'. (2) pShoot pushes the pellet with the raw run.wlevel: game.js:21563 `const w=run.weapon, lv=run.wlevel;` and game.js:21665 `pBullets.push({... kind:'spread', lv});`, so b.lv===4 at level 4 (run.wlevel is clamped 1..5 for non-Cole, e.g. game.js:22301 and 5627). (3) drawBullets' spread branch, game.js:37931, is the first thing tried: `if(p87Draw(_p.rc, b.x, b.y, _p.res, 36+clamp(b.lv||1,1,8)*1.4, b.lv, wlvGlow(b.lv), 1)) continue;`. (4) p87Draw (game.js:36859) calls p87Body (game.js:36852-36856), which reads `WLV_BODY[clamp(lv|0||1,1,8)]` (36853) and returns `xartPalette(P87_SHEET,m)` (36855) for a non-null mode. (5) xartPalette (game.js:39255) black branch is game.js:39269-39270: `x.globalCompositeOperation='multiply'; x.fillStyle='#2a2b31'; x.fillRect(0,0,w,h);`. I measured the actual cells: nca_87 is a playercell rect ["bof_player_ordnance_projectiles_atlas",2383,4,768,768,0,0,768,768] in assets/manifest.js, so XART._touch/_playerCell (game.js:2522-2532) rebuilds a 768x768 canvas and the 192-pitch sx/sy in p87Draw index it correctly. Row 3 cols 0/1/2 of that region measure mean opaque (183,195,197) / (180,193,204) / (182,194,202) with max (255,255,255) — a light silver-white round. Multiplying by #2a2b31 gives (30,33,38): black. So the round is black by construction on a sheet that decoded fine — NOT an XART.rdy() miss. Intent that white is correct is confirmed twice: WLV_GLOW[4] is '#dfe8ff' at game.js:36842 (a white halo, and p87Draw's glow is a separate shadow pass at 36878-36885, so the halo stays white around a black core), and the drop-0812d comment at game.js:36771-36777 quotes Mike giving two lists at once — the tier list "lvl 4 white" and the body list "level 4 could be black" — and the body list is what got coded.
- FIX: In WLV_BODY at assets/game.js line 36844, change `4:'black'` to `4:'white'`. One token. Do not touch WLV_GLOW — '#dfe8ff' at tier 4 (line 36842) is already the right halo — and do not route around xartPalette: its 'white' special case at game.js:39271-39274 ('color' pass plus a 0.34 screen lift) is what stops an achromatic fill desaturating to grey, exactly as the warning comment at game.js:36847-36851 says.
- risk: WLV_BODY is shared, so two side effects, both real. (1) The player muzzle flash also goes through p87Draw at game.js:31209, gated at game.js:31198 on `run.weapon===0 || run.weapon===1`, so the level-4 muzzle flash turns white for BOTH the spread and the machine gun. That is consistent with the tier and it also un-blackens an MG flash that is currently black, but it is a visible change beyond what was reported. (2) WLV_BODY[3] is already 'white' (line 36844), so tiers 3 and 4 would share a body and be told apart only by glow (green #5fe07a vs white #dfe8ff); if that reads as a regression the follow-up is to give tier 3 a green body, not to revert tier 4. Cole's tiers 6-8 and the MG pellet at levels 1-5 are untouched.

### [ADJUSTED] Only the SPREAD looks black at level 4 — the machine gun pellet at the same level looks correctly white — which is why the bug reads as a spread-specific defect rather than a shared palette problem.
- where: game.js:37872
- now: The asymmetry is real and the cited lines are exact, but the conclusion is overstated by one surface. Confirmed: game.js:37871-37872 is `const _cLv=b.lv||1;` / `if(_cLv<=5 && mgcfDraw(b,_cLv)) continue;`, ahead of the p87Draw call on 37873. mgcfDraw (game.js:36828-36832) draws `'mgcf_'+clamp(lv,1,5)+'_'+mgcfFrame(b)` with tint null. Those 30 keys exist and resolve — assets/manifest.js maps mgcf_1_0..mgcf_5_5 into assets/game/atlas/bof_player_ordnance_projectiles.png (mgcf_4_3 = rect 2834,3936,16,20), and that atlas is in XART's PRELOAD regex at game.js:1908 — and they are already colour-graded on disk: I measured frame 3 of each level straight out of the atlas at mean opaque (246,170,78) orange / (94,156,246) blue / (101,246,131) green / (255,255,255) pure white / (246,102,101) red. So for levels 1-5 the MG PELLET returns before reaching p87Draw and never sees WLV_BODY. The spread branch at game.js:37930-37931 has no such guard, so p87Draw is first and the black swap lands there. That is the same 'first path in a fallback chain wins' trap the file documents twice for this code (game.js:37831-37843 for nmg_ shadowing the coloured MG art; 37861-37866 warning nca_87 must go first). WHAT IS WRONG: their claim that "WLV_BODY[4]='black' can never touch [the machine gun]" holds only for the pellet. The MG's MUZZLE FLASH does go through p87Draw at game.js:31209 under the gate at game.js:31198 (`run.weapon===0 || run.weapon===1`), so at level 4 the machine gun's muzzle flash is blackened by the same table today. The symptom is spread-dominant, not strictly spread-only.
- FIX: No second edit. This finding is the explanation for why the symptom reads as spread-only and why a fix aimed at the machine gun pellet would appear to do nothing; the WLV_BODY[4] one-token change above fixes the spread round and, as a bonus, the level-4 muzzle flash on both guns. Do NOT take the alternative of adding `if((b.lv||1)<=5 && mgcfDraw(b,(b.lv||1))) continue;` before game.js:37930 — see risk.
- risk: None if left alone. If the alternative mgcfDraw route were taken for the spread, every pellet would lose its authored diagonal pose (P87_POSE, game.js:36808-36817, measured at +/-46.2 degrees) and its per-shot residual rotation, because mgcfDraw calls drawMfx with ang 0 (game.js:36831) — a level-5 fan would draw seven identical upright pellets travelling sideways to their own flight lines, the exact failure the comments at game.js:37940-37947 and 37967-37968 warn about.

## Boss attack-range / danger-zone / lane-warning indicators

### [UNVERIFIED] The arrows Mike is rejecting. The boss laser telegraph draws marching chevrons along the lane, in a dedicated second pass that exists ONLY to make the arrows read as arrows.
- where: game.js:14601
- now: l23BossBeamDraw (assets/game.js:14501) paints its warn-time telegraph from ONE art key, 'nwarn_lane' (131x235, assets/game/nwarn_lane.png, registered loose in assets/manifest.js as "nwarn_lane":"assets/game/nwarn_lane.png"). It is tiled TWICE inside ctx.rotate(angle - PI/2): pass one (game.js:14595-14598) tiles it seamed to make a continuous corridor; pass two (game.js:14601-14611) re-blits the SAME tile additively, counter-rotated by _back = -(B.angles[i]-PI/2) about each tile centre so the chevrons hold south at every sweep angle, and reveals them one at a time as the rising charge band _gy = len*(1-k) passes each tile. The comment at 14574-14593 records both asks that produced the arrows ("those arrows should be facing south the whole time, and they appear 1 by 1"). Alpha for the corridor is _a = min(1,(a0+a1*k)*pulse*2.35) * hull-overlap ramp * 0.62 (14594-14596); for the arrows it is (.30+.34*k)*(1-_pop*.7)*glow*.9 (14608). Colour is not chosen at all in this branch - the plate's own pixels are used, so the L23_ALERT hue table is ignored whenever the plate is ready.
- FIX: Delete the second (arrow) pass, game.js:14599-14611, and stop drawing 'nwarn_lane' altogether: remove the whole `if(XART.rdy('nwarn_lane')){...}` branch at game.js:14550-14613 plus its procedural `else` at 14614-14623, leaving l23BossBeamDraw responsible only for the beam plate (14509-14518). The telegraph geometry then comes from the new shared cone (see the next finding), not from this function.
- risk: l23BossBeamDraw currently returns true after painting the lane; stage 2/3 beams would have NO telegraph at all for the window between this deletion and the shared cone landing. Do the two edits in one drop.

### [UNVERIFIED] There is no shared danger-zone indicator. Six independent, copy-pasted painters each hardcode their own shape, their own stage-themed colour and their own two-state timing, which is why 'the attack range graphic for ALL bosses is terrible' is one complaint and not six.
- where: game.js:17759
- now: The six are: (1) l23BossBeamDraw's alert lane, game.js:14501/14519-14627 - rotating corridor + arrows, colours from L23_ALERT at 14495-14499; (2) beamRakeDraw, game.js:14232 - the rotating spoke corridor, procedural, warm colour '#ff8a6a' -> live '#ff4d3d' at game.js:14247; (3) sludgeFloodDraw, game.js:15794 - full-height columns; (4) xenoRegentGridDraw, game.js:16517; (5) tidalCascadeDraw, game.js:16642; (6) carrierThunderheadDraw, game.js:17162; plus vileAnnihilationDraw, game.js:45238 - crosshair lines. Every one is gated identically as `if(!X || X.t >= X.tell) return;` so they exist ONLY during the wind-up: nothing is drawn while a boss is idle, so there is no green/idle state anywhere to raise to 25%. Five of the six are already funnelled through ONE place - shipBossDraw calls beamRakeDraw (17759), l23BossBeamDraw (17760), sludgeFloodDraw (17763), tidalCascadeDraw (17765) and carrierMegaDrawUnder->carrierThunderheadDraw (17766/17349) in eight consecutive lines. xenoRegentGridDraw is called from xenoRegentDrawOver (16596) and vileAnnihilationDraw from drawModularBoss (49159). The one hook that reaches EVERY boss kind regardless of renderer is the post-hull block in drawWorld at game.js:54837-54841, which already runs l23WarnSymbolDraw for both `boss` and `subBoss` after the hulls are painted - that placement is the documented fix for the sign being drawn-but-occluded (comment at 54829-54836).

The collector reads the state each system already keeps - b._l23Beam.slots/angles/width/warm, b._brk.n/ang/len/warm, b._s7Flood/_xenoGrid/_s9Cascade/_mega.thunderhead cols+gap, b._annihilation.tx/ty - and returns wedges {x,y,ang,halfArc,len,state} where state is idle | prep | imminent from t vs tell (reuse L23_WARN_RED = 0.60, game.js:14371, as the prep->imminent split). The painter owns the three colours and the 25% idle alpha in ONE place. Then delete the six bodies at 14519-14627, 14232, 15794, 16517, 16642, 17162 and 45238 and their call sites.

Keep its live half and replace only the warm half. Every wedge is drawn inside the world transform at these call sites, so a collector returning screen-space coordinates would land in the wrong place.

## Stage 4 miniboss — OLIVE WARDEN (`olivewarden`): art frames, ammunition, turret muzzle flash, firing pattern

### [UNVERIFIED] Mike: "dont use the projectiles your using for it now." For ~68% of its attack cycle the Warden is not firing machine guns at all — it sits near centre-screen and pumps out fans of the Storm Sovereign's big electric-blue LIGHTNING rounds from its NOSE.

Two of its three modes — 'gate' (13886) and 'drones' (13904) — call `stage4WarfareWall`, which at 13810 fires 8 of 11 columns with role `'spread'` from mount `'C'`. `stage4WarfareShot` (13790) maps role 'spread' → kind `s4rail`, and `drawStage4WarfareProjectile` (33491) draws it as `s4w_spread_round_*` — a 96x96 electric-blue lightning bolt (part of the Storm Sovereign's storm pack), drawn at 38px * S4H_SHOT_DRAW 1.45 = ~55px. Line 13795 even stamps `q._bfam='storm'`, overriding the Warden's own declared `proj:'legion'` family. Timing: 'burst' runs 3.60s (13883), 'gate' 2.40s (13887), 'drones' 5.20s (13905) — so 7.6s of every 11.2s cycle is the blue nose fan. The gold machine-gun round the unit should be firing already exists and is already warmed: role `'mg'` → `s4w_mg_round_*` (6 frames, 33489, registered 2191, warmed 12617).

This function is now called ONLY from the mini's two branches (13886, 13904) — the Storm Sovereign never reaches it (its own tick at 13926 uses 'orb'/'lightning'/'lightningmg' only), so the change is contained to the Warden. Do not touch `stage4WarfareShot`'s role→kind map at 13790; other callers depend on 'spread'.
- risk: 'mg' rounds have a smaller hitbox ([7,18]*1.18 vs [10,22]*1.18) and no `accel/max`, so the wall becomes easier and slower unless the speed at 13810 (3.35) is raised. The visual size drops from ~55px to ~40px, which is the intent but changes readability of the safe lane.

### [UNVERIFIED] Mike: "it needs muzzle flash for its turret." There IS a muzzle flash and it is the right sprite — it is just drawn at a hardpoint that is not on either gun. It blooms on the bare fuselage skirt, roughly 19px inboard and 28px below the actual twin barrels.

The unit's real dual machine-gun barrel clusters are at plate px ~(48,150-190) and ~(186,150-190), and `L:[-0.28,0.18]` / `R:[0.28,0.18]` already sit on that barrel metal (probed alpha 255, rgb 58,57,55 and 5,5,4). The MG_L/MG_R numbers are inherited from a DIFFERENT hull — STORM SOVEREIGN's 512px plate declares `MG_L:[-0.135,0.315]` at 11981, and its comment says those were measured on that master. ### [UNVERIFIED] Mike: "act like its shooting off dual machine gun spread turrets." It never does. The two turrets never fire together and never fire a spread — the machine-gun phase is ONE round per beat alternating left/right, and the only spread in the fight comes out of the nose.

Keep the existing safe-lane logic so the pattern stays dodgeable.

## Stage-5 warp gate run: the dashed route connector, and the Shadow Orb's launch cue peaking 1.7s after the trigger

### [CONFIRMED] Mike: "no lines connecting to the warp gates please, I dont need to travel in those lines, just travel through the gates before they disappear off screen."

Only on-screen segments draw, and they sit beneath the gates and aircraft." game.js:34993 is exactly `ctx.save();ctx.lineWidth=2;ctx.strokeStyle='rgba(105,228,255,.30)';ctx.setLineDash([8,8]);ctx.beginPath();let _routeStarted=false;`. The loop at 34994-34996 walks `s5run.gates[i]` -> `s5run.gates[i+1]`, culls off-screen pairs, then does moveTo/lineTo; 34997 is `ctx.stroke();ctx.restore();`. So a dashed cyan connector is stroked between every consecutive gate centre, beneath the gate sprites (the gate loop starts at 34998). Route data confirmed: S5R_ROUTE = [.28,.54,.76,.23,.54,.29,.69,.18] at game.js:34796, consumed as `x:W*S5R_ROUTE[i%S5R_ROUTE.length]` in s5RunInit's spawn loop at game.js:34805-34808 (function opens at 34802), so the connector zig-zags across the screen and reads as a rail. Uniqueness confirmed: `setLineDash` occurs exactly once in the whole file (grep -c = 1, at 34993). game.js:35191 (riftReturnDraw) and game.js:35229 (s9MapDraw) are the only nearby lineTo calls and both are campaign-map streaks between map nodes, not in-flight. warpFxDraw (34967-34987) draws only slice-refraction and concentric arcs (34985) - no line. TWO NITS that do not change the verdict: (a) `_routeStarted` is dead logic - both branches of `if(!_routeStarted){ctx.moveTo(a.x,a.y);...}else ctx.moveTo(a.x,a.y);` call moveTo, so the path is actually a set of independent segments, not one connected polyline; visually identical, and it still deletes cleanly. (b) s5RunInit is at game.js:34802, not 34801.

In s5RunDraw() delete game.js:34991-34997 (the two comment lines plus the ctx.save()...ctx.stroke();ctx.restore() block). `_routeStarted` is declared and consumed entirely inside it, and all dash/stroke state is inside its own ctx.save()/ctx.restore(), so nothing leaks to the gate loop that begins at 34998. Do not touch s5RunForegroundDraw (game.js:35034) - I read it and it strokes no line, only clips and redraws the near rim (`ctx.rect(g.x-48,g.y+18,96,30); ctx.clip();`).
- risk: Cosmetic only, confirmed by reading the consumers. The pass/miss test in s5RunTick is a pure line-crossing check - game.js:34895 `const _crossers=_crew.filter(s=>g.py < s.y && g.y >= s.y);` and 34898 `Math.abs(s.x-g.x) <= open` against S5R_OPEN (game.js:34794) - and never reads the drawn path. All eight gates exist at once from s5RunInit (34805-34808) with staggered y, so the player can still read the route from the gates themselves. Gate sprites, pass/miss alpha, last-gate glow and the pip counter all draw after 34997 and are untouched.

### [CONFIRMED] Mike: "in space, the shadow orb, you can heard the sound clearly delayed for impact." A previous drop (0903q) already tried to fix this and fixed the wrong sample.
- where: game.js:57647
- now: Confirmed in source AND by re-measuring the WAVs myself; every number they gave reproduced. CODE PATH: game.js:57647 is `spaceShadowRelease:'assets/game/sounds/reviewed_shadow_orb_launch.wav'`. It is fired by spaceShadowRelease() at game.js:21291 `spaceWeaponCue('spaceShadowRelease','shadowRelease')`, which at game.js:21188-21189 resolves the pool and calls `Snd.play(pick,1.72)`. IMPACT IS GENUINELY NOT DEFERRED - I re-traced it: spaceBulletTick's shadowOrb branch at game.js:21555 is `if(hit){b._blastPrimary=hit;spaceShadowBlast(b);}` on the collision frame; spaceShadowBlast (21481) ends at 21492 with `spaceImpact(b,'shadow',b.lv,rad*1.25)`; spaceImpact fires the cue synchronously at game.js:21464-21467. No timer, and unlike the spaceLaser branch (`_launchDelay` at game.js:21500) the orb carries none. MY MEASUREMENTS (48k stereo / 44.1k mono, 100 ms RMS windows): reviewed_shadow_orb_launch.wav = 2250.0 ms, absolute peak at 1744.5 ms, first -12 dB (rel. peak) crossing at 928.4 ms, RMS envelope -26.3 dB @0 ms, dipping to -44.3 dB @300 ms, then swelling continuously to a -9.8 dB plateau held 1400-1900 ms. reviewed_shadow_orb_impact.wav = 1005.8 ms, peak at 60.0 ms, 50% of peak at 4.1 ms - punchy and on time, so drop 0903q's trim did land. explosion_plasma.wav = 1400.0 ms, peak at 454.2 ms (matching the 0903q comment) though it already reaches 50% of peak at 19.3 ms. Orb flight: vy = -(4.15+lv*0.18+p*4.45) px/frame at game.js:21284; with SPACE_SHADOW_MIN_CHARGE=0.42 / SPACE_SHADOW_FULL_CHARGE=1.55 (game.js:21173-21174) p is 0.271-1.0 and lv is 0-5 (spaceWeaponLevel, game.js:21203-21207), giving 5.36-9.50 px/frame = ~321-570 px/s at 60 fps on a VH=512 screen (game.js:14) - sub-second flight. So the loudest event in the Shadow Orb bank arrives ~1.4-1.9 s after the trigger, well after the visible hit. GAIN RATIO CONFIRMED: A.vol = {master:1.0, sfx:0.42} at game.js:57706; launch is c01(1.72 * g1.00 * 0.42 * 1.0) = 0.722 (TAME spaceShadowRelease g:1.00 at 57852), impact is Audio.SFX.spaceShadowHit -> `Snd.play(n)` with no vol (handle built at 58070) = c01(1 * g0.92 * 0.42 * 1.0) = 0.386 (57853); volume math at game.js:57948. That is +5.44 dB on the late swell over the on-contact cue. Both are native:true so A._shape (57886-57887) returns immediately and neither gets WebAudio boost - the ratio holds. The 0903q comment at game.js:57648-57652 is the near-miss: it measured the launch cue's 928 ms -12 dB crossing (exact match to mine) and then re-cut the IMPACT sample.
- FIX: Carried forward. Apply the 0903q treatment to the LAUNCH cue: re-cut assets/game/sounds/reviewed_shadow_orb_launch.wav so its body starts at the plateau - cut at ~1340 ms (60 ms ahead of the -9.8 dB onset at 1400 ms), 4 ms fade-in, normalised - the same recipe used on reviewed_shadow_orb_impact.wav. That yields a ~900 ms file with its transient on the trigger frame and needs no code change if replaced in place. Code-only alternative: repoint `spaceShadowRelease` at game.js:57647 to a front-loaded launch cue and update the comment block at 57648-57652 to record that the launch, not the impact, carried the swell. Do not change spaceImpact (game.js:21436) or the spaceShadowHit mapping at game.js:57653 - I verified both are already correct.
- risk: The launch cue loses its 1.4 s pre-roll swell. Charge tension is preserved: the build-up is a separate loop, spaceShadowCharge -> nsp_bof2_charge_shot.mp3 (game.js:57646), started by Snd.loopOn at game.js:21307 and, because SPACE_SHADOW_AUDIO_DELAY=0 (game.js:21177), it runs for the whole hold. Keep the original file on disk under its old name to A/B. Lowering the launch gain at game.js:57852 instead does NOT close this - the boom still lands late, just quieter.

BROADER SCOPE than reported: setWarpMix is also driven to 1 for the Stage-9 warp at game.js:35072/35079 and the launch cinematic at game.js:54166/54287, so the reflection is not exclusive to the gate run. Do NOT key it off `cfg.native` - that flag is on 23 entries including all the enemy and boss weapons. Do not delete _warpSfx: the comment at game.js:57908-57910 records that it exists because local/file builds cannot route media elements through a ConvolverNode.

The name-set guard is safer than the cfg.native guard, which would silently also de-reverb enemy fire, boss cannons and the flamethrower loops. Verify by ear during an armed gate run, not from source - per this project's own history these audio paths have been misdiagnosed from reading alone twice.

## Freezer fire/ice orb: remove the hold-to-charge mechanic and rename every player-facing string to THERMASHOCK

### [ADJUSTED] The fire/ice orb is a hold-to-charge weapon. While Freezer's TIME FREEZE special is active, holding FIRE winds up a 1.5s charge instead of firing on the normal cadence.
- where: game.js:23010
- now: CONFIRMED AT LINE 23010: `if(k==='freezer' && run && run.weapon===5 && typeof orbIsFire==='function' && orbIsFire()) return true;` inside chargePilotActive() (defined line 23002, and it does return false with no `special` at line 23003). The comment at 23007-23009 records exactly why the clause was added ('Freezer was missing from this ownership gate'). orbIsFire() at line 25009 returns true for both 'fireorb' and 'fireice' (line 25017), and weaponVariant's freezer w===5 arm (lines 5482-5486) returns only 'iceorb' or 'fireice' — never 'fireorb' — so in production a Freezer orbIsFire() is the fireice orb. Returning true sets _chgPilot at line 26032, and the branch at line 26056 swallows the held trigger so the cadence branch at 26057 never calls pShoot(). freezerOrbCharge() is called only from updateSpecial line 22592, and updateSpecial bails at line 22580 (`if(!special) return;`), so the charge exists only while the special is up.
- FIX: In chargePilotActive(), line 23010: add `&& !(typeof orbIsFireIce==='function' && orbIsFireIce())` to the freezer clause. With it false the existing cadence branch at line 26057 runs pShoot() on _weaponCadence().

### [ADJUSTED] Stages 5 and 9 pump the size hardest: the ship doubles on the climb, halves on the brake, then grows again on the settle, ending at 60 on GO.
- where: game.js:54333
- now: THE CITED LINES ARE ALL REAL BUT THE DRAW PATH IS WRONG, SO THE SYMPTOM AND THE TRACE ARE BOTH MIS-STATED. Verified literally: 54309 is `if(run.stage===9){const pe=...;shipH=lerp(14,SPACE_SHIP_SIZE,pe);shipY=...}`; 54311 is `const lift=...; shipH=lerp(62, 128, lift); shipY=...`; 54316 is `if(_space) shipH=lerp(run.stage===9?SPACE_SHIP_SIZE:128,SHIP_DRAW_H,k);`; and the settle lerp at 54324 is indeed unconditional, so it does restart from 62 after the brake arrived at 60. WHAT THE REPORT MISSED: on both space stages shipH is not what reaches the screen. Line 54330 sets `_drawGravityShip=_space&&gravityMode` and line 54334 routes the draw to gravityModeDrawShip(shipX,shipY,_gravityDrawSize); shipH is only the FALLBACK argument used when that returns false. gravityMode is live for essentially the whole entry on both stages: stage 9 calls gravityModeRetain() at drawLaunch init (54164, function at 30653 — it sets phase 'active' immediately), and stage 5 calls gravityModeStart() during run at _s5arrival>=0.10 (54176). _gravityDrawSize (54331-54333) is a THIRD, parallel expression: lerp(14,SPACE_SHIP_SIZE,...) for stage 9's run, SPACE_SHIP_SIZE for the countdown, and Math.max(SPACE_SHIP_SIZE, shipH) otherwise. And PLAY on those stages also short-circuits into the same renderer at default size — `if(gravityMode && gravityModeDrawShip()) return;` (30906) with `size=drawSize||SPACE_SHIP_SIZE` (30722). CORRECTED TRACE, stage 5: 62 (plain hull, pre-gravity) -> 128 (run) -> 60 (brake) -> 62 -> 76.1 (settle) -> 76.1 (the 'gravity' phase, 54209-54211, falls to the else at 54328) -> 48 (cd) -> 48 (PLAY). Stage 9: 14 -> 48 (run) -> 48 -> 60 (brake) -> 62 -> 76.1 (settle) -> 48 (cd) -> 48 (PLAY). So there is no 60 and no drop at GO on these stages; the real discontinuity is a ~37% collapse from 76.1 to 48 at the settle-to-countdown boundary, driven by the unconditional settle lerp at 54324 feeding Math.max(SPACE_SHIP_SIZE, shipH) at 54333 and then being discarded when the cd branch pins SPACE_SHIP_SIZE.
- FIX: Minimal and consistent with the other two fixes: change the settle literal at 54324 to `shipH=lerp(SHIP_DRAW_H, POSE.h, k);` (already proposed above). With POSE.h also fixed to SHIP_DRAW_H, shipH holds 60 through the settle, so _gravityDrawSize at 54333 becomes Math.max(48,60)=60 and the cd drop shrinks from 76.1->48 to 60->48. To remove the space pop entirely, change the `ph==='cd'` arm of _gravityDrawSize at line 54333 from `SPACE_SHIP_SIZE` to `Math.max(SPACE_SHIP_SIZE, shipH)` so the countdown holds whatever the brake/settle arrived at. Do NOT apply the report's fix of deleting the height lerps at 54309/54311/54316 — 54309's is already inert on screen and 54311/54316 feed the authored 128px climb.
- risk: Touching the cd arm at 54333 changes the size the Gravity craft holds through 3-2-1 on both space stages, which is the beat that currently matches PLAY exactly — so it fixes the settle pop at the cost of introducing a 60 -> 48 pop at GO unless PLAY's default is changed too. Safer option: leave 54333 alone and take only the 54324 fix, which reduces the pop without moving the GO seam. The 128px climb at 54311 reads as deliberate staging ("the ship scales up as it rockets toward space", 54310) so it is a product call, not a bug. Also note gravityModeDrawShip returns false when spaceAtlasCanvas('ship_base',pilot) is missing (30724), which silently falls back to drawShipSprite at shipH — an art-readiness fallback that draws a completely different craft at a different size.

## Player ship turning/banking sprite selection — input direction to bank frame

### [UNVERIFIED] Lizzie's ship faces the opposite way from the direction being held: press RIGHT and her prop plane is drawn nosed up-LEFT, press LEFT and she is nosed up-RIGHT. Affects both the soft/steep lean (pv reel) and the full edge-on twist (br reel), on every stage that uses the normal player draw path.
- where: game.js:46817
- now: Input maps cleanly: assets/game.js:25968 sets dir=-1 for 'left' and +1 for 'right', and assets/game.js:25981 ramps player._bank toward it, so left input => _bank<0 and right input => _bank>0. _shipFrameKey (assets/game.js:30381) then picks the frame through two per-pilot handedness tables: `right=(SHIP_BANK_FLIP[pk] ? bank<0 : bank>0)` (line 30387) and `rightBr=(SHIP_TWIST_FLIP[pk] ? bank<0 : bank>0)` (line 30388), consumed at line 30405 (`_br6` if rightBr else `_br2`) and line 30406 (`_pv3`/`_pv1` under |bank|<0.5, else `_pv4`/`_pv0`). Lizzie appears in NEITHER table, so holding right resolves right=true -> _pv3/_pv4 and rightBr=true -> _br6. Her art is authored mirrored relative to the other pilots, exactly like the four that DO carry the flag. Measured directly off the atlas (assets/game/atlas/bof_player_ships_barrel_rolls.png, cells from BOFX.ships in assets/manifest.js), as (nose_x - centroid_x)/hull_width, + meaning the nose is swung right: lizzie pv0 +0.025, pv1 +0.014, pv3 -0.193, pv4 -0.166, br2 +0.227, br6 -0.227. Her separate cutscene pair is inverted the same way (ship_lizzie_l fuselage axis +0.074 = nose right, ship_lizzie_r -0.131 = nose left) while all eight other pilots have _l nose-left and _r nose-right. Confirmed visually at 2x: ship_lizzie_pv1 and ship_lizzie_l show the propeller swung up-RIGHT; ship_lizzie_pv3, ship_lizzie_r and ship_lizzie_br6 show it up-LEFT. So _pv3/_pv4/_br6 are her LEFT-turn frames and the code hands them to right input. The reason she was missed is recorded in the file itself: the drop 0903y table at assets/game.js:46836-46845 reads her lean as '(weak)' (pv0 -.061, pv4 -.036, both negative) and her twist as br2 -.013 / br6 +.013, then concludes 'her twist reads cleanly (br6 right) so she is out of this one too'. Those twist numbers are effectively zero; the real separation is +/-0.227, larger than maverick's 0.107 and cole's 0.119 which that same table treated as decisive. The sign of a null reading was taken as signal.
- FIX: Add `lizzie:1` to both tables: `const SHIP_BANK_FLIP={axel:1, decker:1, freezer:1, yuri:1, lizzie:1};` at assets/game.js:46817 and `const SHIP_TWIST_FLIP={axel:1, decker:1, juggernaut:1, lizzie:1};` at assets/game.js:46851. No change to _shipFrameKey itself.
- risk: SHIP_BANK_FLIP also steers _shipThrRig (assets/game.js:30409), which looks the thruster row up by the resolved frame suffix - it follows the frame, so the plume moves with the hull, but Lizzie's plume anchor should be re-eyeballed at full bank since hers is the one entry with flip:true in THRUSTER_MOUNTS. The SHIP_TWIST_FLIP half is invisible while she is mounted: lzMountActive clamps her bank to +/-0.45 (assets/game.js:25979), below the TWIST threshold of 0.82. Her authored _l/_r cutscene pair (used by drawShipSprite, assets/game.js:53915) is mirrored the same way and has no flip support, so cutscene banking stays wrong - a separate fix, not covered by these two table entries.

### [UNVERIFIED] In Gravity/space mode - stages 5 and 9 - every pilot's ship turns the wrong way: hold left and the craft yaws right, hold right and it yaws left. This path bypasses the per-pilot flip tables entirely, so it is wrong for all nine pilots, not just some.
- where: game.js:30783
- now: _drawPlayerCore short-circuits at assets/game.js:30906 (`if(gravityMode && gravityModeDrawShip()) return;`), so in stages 5 and 9 (gravityModeStart gates on `run.stage!==5 && run.stage!==9`, assets/game.js:30643) none of _shipFrameKey, SHIP_BANK_FLIP or SHIP_TWIST_FLIP runs. gravityModeDrawShip instead does its own pick at assets/game.js:30782-30783: `const step=ab<0.34?1:(ab<0.68?2:3); const bankKey='ship_bank_'+(bank<0?'l':'r')+step;` - it trusts the atlas frame names literally. The names are inverted relative to the art they hold. Measured from assets/game/atlas/bof_gravity_mode_space_weapons.png with rects from assets/game/atlas/bof_gravity_mode_space_weapons.json, as (nose_x - centroid_x)/hull_width: ship_bank_l1 +0.278, l2 +0.353, l3 +0.357 (nose swung RIGHT) and ship_bank_r1 -0.274, r2 -0.398, r3 -0.263 (nose swung LEFT), against ship_base 0.000. Confirmed visually at 3x: ship_bank_l3 has its nose cone at the upper right with the engine bell at the lower left, and ship_bank_r3 is its mirror. Since left input gives bank<0 (assets/game.js:25968, 25981), holding LEFT selects ship_bank_l*, which is the right-turn art. This is one shared airframe recoloured per pilot via spaceAtlasDraw's pal argument, which is why it presents as 'my ships' plural rather than one pilot, and stage 9 is where all recent work has been.

The sibling roll reel in the same block (ship_roll_*, assets/game.js:30776, `player.roll.dir>0?16-fi:fi`) is untouched - its 17 frames do not read as a monotonic rotation and its handedness was not verified here.

## enemy-render: procedural vector overlays + broad purple "void" FX from stage 4 onward

### [UNVERIFIED] Purple MS-Paint triangle drawn ON every damaged Stage-5 enemy hull (also Stage 8/9 via the same function)
- where: game.js:43291
- now: drawSpaceDamage(e,frac,w,h,s9) at line 43289 fires for any hull at frac<=0.66. Its last statement is a flat three-point vector flame: `const fy=h*.13+Math.sin(t*14)*3; ctx.fillStyle=s9?'#48eaff':'#d248ff'; ctx.beginPath(); ctx.moveTo(-5,fy); ctx.lineTo(Math.sin(t*10)*3,fy-15-Math.sin(t*20)*4); ctx.lineTo(6,fy); ctx.fill();` — #d248ff is bright violet. It is called unconditionally from drawS5Space (line 43313, `drawSpaceDamage(e,frac,dw,dh,false)`) for all eleven S5SPACE hulls (s5frigate, s5rammer, s5skimmer, s5gravity, s5interceptor, s5minelayer, s5satellite, s5portalmine, s5repair, s5leech, s5station) and from drawS9Void (line 43537, s9=true, cyan). The blobs above it (ctx.arc triples in rgba(18,21,30,.92)) are flat vector smoke. This is NOT an XART.rdy fallback — it runs even when the authored art is present. Worse, the authored smoke reel drawEnemyDamage (line 28283) ALSO runs on these units from the world loop at line 54812, because it only early-returns on `e._nef` (set solely at line 35302 for the S9 void roster) and DMG_TIER (line 28186) starts at f<0.65 — the same threshold. So a damaged Stage-5 hull carries the authored nsd_diss/nsd_chim plume AND a hand-drawn purple triangle plus grey circles at once, which is exactly the 'two plumes from two different systems at two different scales' failure the comment at drawEnemyDamage line 28285 warns against.
- FIX: In drawSpaceDamage (game.js:43289), add one guard as the first statement after the frac test: `if(typeof XART!=='undefined'&&XART.rdy('nsd_chim_0'))return;` so the procedural overlay is only a fallback for when the authored damage reel has not decoded. Do not touch the callers.
- risk: Between 0.15 and 0.66 HP the authored tier is nsd_diss/nsd_chim (smoke only, no flame), so damaged Stage-5/8/9 hulls lose the orange/violet fire cue until they drop under 0.15 where nxp_upward adds fire. If nsd_chim_0 is registered but nxp_upward_* is not, burning units would show smoke only. Also removes the only damage cue on any unit whose hull art loaded before the nsd_ reel did.

Stage 6: s6wind and s6bolt both laser, s6missile and s6seeker both missile, s6orb void. (b.t||0)*1.65 : atan2(vy,vx)-PI/2`, i.e. every void-family round spins on its own clock instead of facing its travel direction — an aimed s4bomb or s6orb tumbles. The comment above the table at 33574-33577 claims 'Generic ammunition is upgraded only when the shot is boss-owned', but bossProjectileFxFamily (33593) checks BPFX_STAGE_PROJECTILE before the `b._boss` test, so it applies to every enemy's fire.

For a placement that cannot end up half off-world, seed homeX from the world instead of the camera: worldWidth()*.5+side*168 (worldWidth() is at 1366 and returns 680 here, so that gives 172 and 508 - both comfortably inside the plate).
- risk: Their risk analysis is correct and I confirmed each hook. A world-latched helper can leave the 480px window (camX 0..200 over a 680 world), so at one extreme a helper is off-screen and cannot be shot or shoot fairly. Its hit tests all read t.x/t.y so they stay correct: stage4CoreTurretAt (13506), BeamAt (13512), FlameAt (13520). stage4CoreSpreadOffsets (13618-13626, was 13603-13611) does assume both helpers hold the outer lanes when it narrows the carrier's fan to [-.14,0,.14], so an off-screen helper leaves that fan narrow for no visible reason. And since spawn fires at the 50% threshold (13684, was 13669) with the camera wherever the player left it, two runs place the helpers differently - use the worldWidth() seed if a repeatable arena matters.

### [CONFIRMED] The boss's OWN twin chainguns - the ones with a real 360-degree calibration sweep already authored - never deploy and are never drawn.
- where: game.js:13753
- now: Confirmed exactly, with the +15 drift. S.finalGuns is initialised to 'off' at 13411 (was 13396). The single assignment in the whole file, S.finalGuns='active' at 13759 (was 13744), sits INSIDE the if(S.finalGuns==='deploying') guard opened at 13753 - so nothing anywhere writes 'deploying', the guard can never open, and finalGuns is 'off' for the entire fight. stage4FinalChaingunTick (fn at 13853, called every frame at 13944, their 13929) therefore returns on its first line, 13854. That kills the whole 25% phase: the twin-socket tracking guns (13862-13868), the 4-round lightning cadence (13869-13882) and specifically the full-TAU deploy sweep at 13857-13859 (turn=ease*TAU; aimL=PI/2-turn, aimR=PI/2+turn) - the one genuine 360-degree chaingun rotation authored in this boss. I verified the negative claims rather than trusting them: `grep -rn "finalGuns *= *'deploying'"` over assets/ and _BUILD_SOURCE/ returns nothing, and `git log --oneline -S"finalGuns='deploying'" -- assets/game.js` returns nothing, so this was never wired rather than broken later. Independently, there is no draw: game.js contains exactly three occurrences of s4w_final_chaingun - the two registrations at 2181-2182 and the decode warm-up at 12615 - so even if the phase ran the guns would fire invisible rounds off a hull with no visible weapon. The art itself is fine and fully resolvable (loose PNGs on disk plus atlas cells in assets/game/atlas/stage_runtime_atlases.js).
- FIX: Unchanged, line numbers corrected. Two one-liners, in this order. (1) Arm it: in stage4ShieldBeginRearm, beside the existing 13684 (if(threshold<=.501)stage4CoreTurretSpawnMissing(b,threshold);), add if(threshold<=.251&&S.finalGuns==='off'){S.finalGuns='deploying';S.finalGunT=0;}. I checked the timing works: the deploy advance S.finalGunT+=dt at 13754 runs inside if(H.rearming), and rearming ends at rearmT>=1.08 (13763) while the promotion needs finalGunT>=.92 (13755), so the sweep completes inside the rearm window and 13753-13761 promotes it to 'active' as written. The comment at 13682-13683 confirms the 25% rearm is where this phase was meant to live. (2) Draw it: in stage4WarfareDrawOver add a block that, when S.finalGuns!=='off', draws s4w_final_chaingun_<Math.floor(S.finalGunSpin)%8> at each stage4FinalGunMount(b,side) (13831-13835, was 13816-13820) rotated by stage4FinalGunAngle(b,side)-PI/2, each behind its own XART.rdy() guard. The mount's own comment (13832-13834) gives the geometry: core 110px in game, socketOffset=(92/224)*110, centre (b.x, drawY+21).
- risk: Their risk statement is right. This turns a dead phase back on, so the 25% fight gets materially harder - two extra tracking chainguns at .060-.073s cadence (13871) on top of whatever helpers survived. Verify against the balance the 0905 helper drops settled on, and gate behind a phase check if it proves too much. Part (2) has no existing draw to regress, so it is safe to land alone: adding the draw while leaving finalGuns 'off' shows nothing today, but adding the draw and arming only in a debug toggle lets Mike see the 360-degree sweep with no difficulty change. Note one thing they did not: the sweep at 13857-13859 returns before the aim tracking, so during those 0.92s the guns are pointing wherever the sweep put them and stage4FinalGunTip feeds nothing (no rounds fire while 'deploying'), so the sweep is purely visual and cannot spawn stray shots.

The rendered plate shows the pointed nose/cockpit at the TOP and the twin exhaust nozzles plus delta tailfins at the BOTTOM; the jungle cruiser renders the other way up. It is vertical and 180 degrees backwards. The engine's own convention agrees: shipBossDraw's not-ready fallback silhouette draws its nose at +y with ctx.moveTo(0, h*0.50) (game.js:17754), i.e. nose DOWN at the player. CORRECTION 1 - THE ROT MECHANISM THEY CITE NO LONGER EXISTS. game.js was edited during this verification (mtime 2026-09-06 00:29, md5 a629617ceb71ad0142b9c3c0a603254e, +15 lines); a 0906 pass titled 'BOSSES DO NOT BANK, ROLL OR FLIP' (game.js:12966-12971, Mike: 'do not ever turn bosses like this, ever ... never flip or do janky css stuff') replaced `let x=0,y=0,rot=wall?0:(b._sbaBank||0)*0.055` with `let x=0,y=0,rot=0` at game.js:12972 and disabled the roll's negative scale.x at 12990-12997. Walking the current function to its return at 13025, blacksteel matches NO rot branch (not _mwAttack, not junglecruiser, not _s4war), so P.rot is exactly 0 and P.sx/sy are 1 apart from millisecond attack pulses. The plate is blitted axis-aligned and unrotated at game.js:17837. The reversal is therefore 100% baked into the source art - an even cleaner result than they reported. RAP_AEROBATICS=false confirmed at its new line game.js:15104, and blacksteelManoeuvre (15164) is only reachable from behind that flag (15220). CORRECTION 2 - THE MOUNT COROLLARY IS BACKWARDS (see risk). Corroboration from the mounts stands: shipBossMount computes ly=b.h*M[1] with +y down (game.js:12709) and every act fires at Math.PI/2 (game.js:15226, 15231, 15234, 15245, 15246). I probed the plate's alpha at the exact mount points: C [0,+0.43] lands on canvas (256,476) with alpha=0 and only 17/81 ink in a 9x9 - the notch between the two exhaust nozzles. The nose gun genuinely fires from the tail.
- FIX: Unchanged and correct: art-only. Rotate the four PNGs in assets/game/stage6_raptor/ 180 degrees in place on their shared 512 canvas so the nose points SOUTH. Do NOT add a rotation or a ctx.scale(1,-1) in shipBossDraw - the 0906 header at game.js:12966-12971 rules out flipping bosses in code in Mike's own words, which is a far better citation than the game.js:17765 comment they used (that one forbids draw-time RECOLOURING, not geometry). Their alternative citation to audit_enemy_facing_0905.py's 'IT REPORTS, IT DOES NOT ROTATE' header is real and also supports the art-side fix.
- risk: THEIR RISK NOTE IS HALF WRONG AND WOULD REINTRODUCE THE BUG IF FOLLOWED. I probed both plates. On the 180-rotated plate the mounts resolve as: C [0,+0.43] -> canvas (256,476), ink 81/81 - dead on the nose. So C:[0,0.43] at game.js:12256 is ALREADY CORRECT and must be LEFT ALONE; changing it to C:[0,-0.43] as they propose puts the nose gun back on the tail. Only L/R need mirroring: as authored, L/R [+-0.25,+0.12] -> canvas (128/384,317) which is 81/81 ink on the CURRENT north plate but 0/81 empty air after the flip, while [+-0.25,-0.12] is 0/81 now and 81/81 after. So the single correct mount edit at game.js:12256 is L:[-0.25,0.12]->[-0.25,-0.12] and R:[0.25,0.12]->[0.25,-0.12], C untouched. Also note the 180 rotation shifts the alpha bbox one pixel in x (72..439 -> 73..440) because 512 is even and the ink is not perfectly centred; negligible but it means the four plates must all be rotated the same way in the same pass. blacksteelHit's wing routing (game.js:15148) is x-only and is genuinely unaffected, as they said.

### [CONFIRMED] The raptor is far too big - its hull fills roughly half the visible width and two thirds of the screen height, and its square hitbox extends ~48px of empty air past each wingtip.
- where: game.js:12255
- now: CONFIRMED, arithmetic independently reproduced. game.js:12255 reads w:340,h:340 and the comment above it at 12252-12254 records the 0904k doubling from 170. shipBossInit copies it verbatim (b.w=D.w; b.h=D.h, game.js:12587) and shipBossDraw sizes the blit from it (const s=b.w/256, w=256*s, h=(b.h>0?b.h:256*s), now game.js:17810; blit at 17837), so the whole 512 canvas maps into a 340x340 box. Their no-multiplier claim holds: ENEMY_ART_FOOT=2.15 lives at game.js:31679 and is read only by drawNewEnemyArt (31648, function starts 31635) and the stage-9 helper (34753), neither of which a _ship subBoss reaches; the blacksteel branch in shipBossInit (game.js:12641-12646) only preloads FX keys and sets no _foot. I measured the alpha bbox myself as 367x492 in the 512 canvas, so drawn ink is 340*367/512 = 243.7 wide by 340*492/512 = 326.7 tall, against VW=480 / VH=512 (game.js:14): 50.8% and 63.8%. Hitbox slop confirmed: 340/2 - 243.7/2 = 48.1px of transparent canvas per side counts as hull, read by the bomb proximity test (game.js:27378), the laser test (27446) and the player body-collision test (28037), all of which use subBoss.w/2. CORRECTIONS, all minor: (a) game.js was edited mid-verification and several of their lines drifted +15 - MINIBOSS_HP_FLOOR is game.js:17887 (not 17888), the lane clamp is 15202, blacksteelHit's half is 15148; (b) heralddeath is game.js:12241, not 12240; (c) 'every other miniboss is roughly half that' overstates it - they missed dualscoopdredger at w:240,h:220 (game.js:12183), which is the next-largest mini. 340 is still the largest by a clear margin and the only one over 256. The HP point is right: MINIBOSS_HP_FLOOR[5]=1260 dominates the row's hp:245.
- FIX: Unchanged: edit the single row at game.js:12255, w:340,h:340 -> about w:230,h:230. Everything downstream derives from b.w and follows automatically - the mounts are fractions of the box, blacksteelHit uses (b.w||340)*0.5 (game.js:15148) and the lane clamp uses (b.w||340)*0.28 (game.js:15202). If the intent is 'biggest mini on the roster', anything from 230 to 250 clears dualscoopdredger's 240 without being an outlier.
- risk: As they state. HP is untouched because the stage-6 floor of 1260 (game.js:17887) already overrides hp:245. The smaller hull is a genuinely harder target and lengthens the stage-6 fight - a balance change worth showing Mike. The two stale '340' fallback literals at game.js:15148 and 15202 only fire if b.w is missing, but should be updated in the same edit. NOTE THE INTERACTION WITH FINDING 1: shrinking the box also shrinks the mount offsets proportionally, so do the size change and the art/mount fix together and re-check the muzzle positions once, not twice.

### [CONFIRMED] The raptor does not read as 16-bit pixel art - it is a smooth continuous-tone render sitting next to hand-authored sprites, and it visibly changes art style from the rest of the stage.
- where: game.js:2068
- now: CONFIRMED by independent measurement. The registration comment at game.js:2068-2071 does say 'SpriteCook, 0904k'. Counting unique RGB over pixels with alpha>200 only (the opaque-only method CLAUDE.md:400-406 and the normalize script's docstring both insist on): nsb_rap_intact 16,142 colours / 3,884 semi-transparent px, nsb_rap_wl 17,447, nsb_rap_wr 18,910, nsb_rap_crit 20,799 - all 512x512. The authored 256x256 hulls beside them: nsb_jungle_cruiser 60 colours and ZERO semi-transparent pixels, nsb_siege_ember 66 / 0, nsb_void_bat 66 / 0. My numbers differ from theirs by a few hundred (different alpha threshold) but the two-to-three-orders-of-magnitude gap is identical. This is exactly the trap CLAUDE.md:400-402 records from the 0905 Cryo Spear job ('61 colours ... 19,063 ... a continuous-tone render at sprite resolution'), and _BUILD_SOURCE/normalize_spritecook_plate_0905.py exists to fix it - I read its docstring and it snaps every opaque pixel to a reference palette with dither off and solves the pivot by alpha-mask IoU, as they describe. assets/game/stage6_raptor/ holds only the four PNGs, and grepping docs/ for nsb_rap / stage6_raptor / 0904k returns one unrelated hit (docs/BACKLOG_0904.md:19, the aerobatics flag), so no normalization pass is recorded. Their draw-path point is right and I confirmed the line: shipBossDraw sets ctx.imageSmoothingEnabled=false at game.js:17833 (their 17817, drifted), so the blit is already nearest-neighbour and the softness is in the source pixels. One thing they did not say that matters: the ~3,900-4,300 semi-transparent edge pixels per plate are themselves un-pixel-art - the authored hulls have exactly zero - so the anti-aliased silhouette edge needs hard-cutting in the same pass, not just the interior palette.
- FIX: Unchanged: run the four plates through _BUILD_SOURCE/normalize_spritecook_plate_0905.py against an authored 60-66 colour BOF hull (nsb_jungle_cruiser.png or nsb_siege_ember.png) with dither off, using the SAME reference for all four so they stay on one palette and one footprint. Add an alpha hard-threshold so the ~4k semi-transparent edge pixels per plate collapse to on/off like every authored hull. If it still reads soft, regenerate at 256x256 via SpriteCook using edit_asset_id (not reference_asset_id) from the corrected intact plate, then normalize.
- risk: As they state, plus one ordering constraint. A 60-66 colour lock will flatten the render's gradients; all four damage states must be normalized against the SAME reference and re-checked for mutual silhouette IoU or the jet will jump the frame a wing breaks - the exact failure the game.js:2068-2071 comment says the plates were aligned to prevent. Re-run _BUILD_SOURCE/black_edge_bosses_0904o.py afterwards. CRITICAL SEQUENCING: do the 180-degree facing rotation from finding 1 FIRST, then normalize, then re-measure the alpha bbox - shipBossDraw places the plate by its whole canvas, so any footprint change moves the hull relative to both its mounts and its square hitbox, and doing these passes in the other order means measuring the bbox twice.

The 'a kind name is not an art prefix' warning is at game.js:24845-24847. Nothing else needs verifying before the delete. Sequence it after the art work in findings 1 and 3 so a single stage-6 spawn confirms nsb_rap_intact is ready on the first drawn frame.

### [CONFIRMED] The sound a player shot makes when it hits the stage-4 boss's shield is wrong and grating — a generic laser-tick sample, retriggered at full volume on every round with no throttle.
- where: game.js:13738
- now: Verified line by line. stage4ShieldDeflectBullet (now assets/game.js:13715-13738) ends at 13738 with `stage4WarfareSound('shieldHit','shieldHitLight');shake=Math.max(shake,1.5);return true;`. `grep -n "shieldHit[^LH]"` returns exactly ONE hit in the whole file — that line — so the primary name `shieldHit` is not defined anywhere in Audio.SFX, and stage4WarfareSound (13769: `const fn=Audio.SFX[primary]||Audio.SFX[fallback];if(fn)fn();`) always falls through to `shieldHitLight`. That fallback resolves to assets/game/sounds/shield_hit_light.wav, registered in BOFA.sfx at 57695 — the SAME file already bound to `laserBeamHit` (57645) and `spaceLaserHit` (57660), i.e. the engine's generic laser-hit tick, not a shield cue. Aggravating factor (a) CONFIRMED and verified directly: A.TAME spans 57781-57894 and contains no `shieldHitLight` entry (the only 'shield' key in it is megaShieldPickup at 57884). A.play (57947-57949) reads `const cfg=A.TAME[name]; if(cfg && cfg.min){...retrigger throttle...}` and `const gm=cfg?cfg.g:1`, so with no TAME row the sample is neither gain-shaped nor rate-limited — one full-volume retrigger per deflected bullet. The sibling path stage4ShieldAbsorbHit does gate itself: 13711 `if(H.sfx<=0){H.sfx=.10;stage4WarfareSound(H.rearming?'bossShieldStatic':'shieldDeflect','enemyElectricBolt');}`. Aggravating factor (b) CONFIRMED: purpose-built cues exist and resolve — `shieldImpact`/`shieldImpactHeavy` are synth functions at 6293/6297 (header comment 6285-6292 records Mike asking for "the impact cue, distorted and pitch-shifted"), and `bossShieldStatic`/`shieldDeflect` at 6368/6369 sit under the comment labelled "STORM SOVEREIGN SHIELD KIT" at 6361. Neither name appears in BOFA.sfx, so the sample-override loop at 58085 (`for(const m in BOFA.sfx){Audio.SFX[m]=...Snd.play(n)}`) leaves them as synth and they are genuinely callable.
- FIX: Carried forward unchanged, with the line renumbered. In stage4ShieldDeflectBullet at assets/game.js:13738 replace `stage4WarfareSound('shieldHit','shieldHitLight');` with `if(H.sfx<=0){H.sfx=.06;stage4WarfareSound('shieldImpact','shieldDeflect');}` — `H` is already in scope from the function's own first line (13716). Substitute 'shieldDeflect' as the primary if Mike prefers the sharp electric snap over the heavy absorbed thud. No other line changes.
- risk: H.sfx is shared with stage4ShieldAbsorbHit (13711), so gating the deflect cue on it also mildly rate-limits the node-hit cue; use a separate field (H.dsfx) if that matters. shieldImpact is a synth cue built from toneDist rather than a .wav, so it will be quieter than the sample it replaces and may need a level pass. Note the flip side of the current bug: shieldHitLight has no TAME gain entry, so it plays at gm=1 — the replacement will sound quieter than a naive comparison suggests.

### [CONFIRMED] The blue lightning attack fires rarely, and can never fire while the shield is up, which is most of the fight.
- where: game.js:13973
- now: Verified in full. The attack is the terminal `else` branch of stage4WarfareBossTick (function at 13941; lightning branch 13988-14005), NOT the SHIPBOSS pattern `s4lightning` — and that pattern really is dead for this boss: it is declared in `pats` at 11984, its slots at 12805 and its profile row `s4lightning:{tell:0.64,recover:0.32,kick:11,shake:5}` at 12845, but every route to shipBossAttack is gated on fireCd (12914 via shipBossQueueAttack, 12956 via _sba, 19032 subBossAttack, 29441 bossAttack__inner reached only from 29195/29305 which both test `if(b.fireCd<=0)`), and 13942 sets b.fireCd=999 every frame. The mode loop is fixed with no randomisation: mode exits are `if(S.t>=4.25)...'flyaway'` at 13973, `if(S.t>=3.55)...'swerve'` at 13981, `if(S.t>=1.72)...'lightning'` at 13986, `if(S.t>=4.12)...'burst'` at 14005 — 4.25+3.55+1.72+4.12 = 13.64 s of scaled mode time per lightning window. Inside lightning only three bolt volleys fire (13990 wings, 13994 wings, 13998 centre lance) plus one orb (14002). Shield gating CONFIRMED: the 'shield' branch's only handoff is 13954 `if(!H||!H.active){stage4WarfareSetMode(b,'burst');}` — while H.active is true the boss stays in 'shield' indefinitely, firing only orbs at 13958. The shield starts active (stage4WarfareInit 13427 `shield={active:true,...}`), re-arms at the 75/50/25% thresholds declared at 13410 and tripped by stage4WarfareAfterDamage (13696-13701), and rearm loops straight back via 13765 `stage4WarfareSetMode(b,'shield')`. Only stage4ShieldDestroyNode's all-four-dead branch (13670-13672) breaks out to 'burst'. Net: zero lightning while the shield is up; one short window per 13.6 s while it is down.
- FIX: Carried forward with the line renumbered. One-line cadence change in stage4WarfareBossTick: at assets/game.js:13973 make the burst exit alternate into lightning while the shield is down — `if(S.t>=4.25)stage4WarfareSetMode(b,(S.shield&&!S.shield.active&&!S.shield.rearming&&(S.round&1))?'lightning':'flyaway');`. S.shield always exists on this path (stage4WarfareBossTick is the non-mini tick; stage4WarfareInit 13425-13433 builds the shield for every non-mini). If more is wanted, additionally shorten the lightning branch's event gaps at 13989/13993/13997 (1.12/1.68/2.28) rather than adding new events.
- risk: Skipping 'flyaway' on alternate rounds also skips the two shootable orbs spawned at 13979 and the swerve reposition at 13983-13986, so the boss holds centre more and the fight loses orb pressure. S.round is bumped in stage4WarfareSetMode (13773) on EVERY mode change, so the (S.round&1) alternation is stable but shared with rearm/shield transitions — a mid-cycle re-arm will flip its parity.

### [ADJUSTED] The projectiles the stage-4 boss itself fires read as small; the drawn size levers are per-call and the boss's own rounds sit below 1.0 on every one of them.
- where: game.js:13802
- now: Core mechanism CONFIRMED. stage4WarfareShot (13803-13815) computes hitbox and drawn size independently: `const _sz=role==='orb'?[28,28]:(role==='lightning'?[13,30]:(role==='spread'?[10,22]:[7,18]));` at 13806, `const size=[Math.round(_sz[0]*S4H_SHOT_HIT), Math.round(_sz[1]*S4H_SHOT_HIT)];` at 13807, then 13808-13809 `eShootT(...,{w:opt.w||size[0],h:opt.h||size[1],silent:true,szMul:(opt.szMul||1)*S4H_SHOT_DRAW})`. Constants at 13802: `const S4H_SHOT_DRAW=1.45, S4H_SHOT_HIT=1.18;` under the drop-0905c comment 13788-13801. eShootT (36061-36075) confirms the split: 36066 stores `w:opts.w||7,h:opts.h||7` as the hitbox and 36067 stores `szMul:opts.szMul` untouched; drawStage4WarfareProjectile (33494) then multiplies the per-role ART height by b.szMul — 33504 mg h=28, 33505 lightningmg h=34, 33506 spread h=38, 33507 orb h=62, 33508 lance h=54, applied at 33509 (`const im=XART.get(key),mul=b.szMul||1,w=h*(...)*mul;h*=mul;`), and the 'lightning' bolt goes through combatAtlasDraw at 62*mul x 108*mul (33501). The carrier's own per-call szMul values are all below 1 as claimed: 13958 orb .76, 13968 burst chaingun .78, 13979 flyaway orb .80, 14002 centre orb .92 — 1.10/1.13/1.16/1.33 effective, so a burst round draws 34*.78*1.45 = 38.4 px and a shield orb 62*.76*1.45 = 68.3 px. The three lightning volleys are already large (1.18/1.22/1.68 at 13990/13994/13998).
- FIX: Carried forward with lines renumbered and the rationale corrected. Raise the per-call `szMul` for the carrier's own weapons rather than the shared constants: assets/game.js:13958 (.76), 13968 (.78), 13979 (.80), 14002 (.92) — e.g. multiply each by ~1.4. Add 13637 (core-turret spread, .92) if that weapon reads small too. Leave `S4H_SHOT_DRAW` at 13802 alone — but for the correct reason: it is the shared funnel for the Olive Warden's eleven-column wall (13825, which passes NO szMul and therefore already draws at the full 1.45) as well as the helpers, so moving it would resize the Warden's wall, which nobody complained about. If the hitbox should grow with the art, bump the per-role `_sz` base at 13806 instead of S4H_SHOT_HIT so only the carrier's roles move.
- risk: Drawn size and hitbox are deliberately decoupled (comment 13794-13800 records the reasoning); enlarging only the draw widens the gap between what the player sees and what kills them. Enlarging the orbs also enlarges the shootable ones (`shootable:true` at 13958/13979/14002), making them easier to snipe — a small difficulty change in the player's favour. Separately, drawStage4WarfareProjectile returns false at 33509 when `!XART.rdy(key)`, and its caller at 38074 then falls through to the generic bullet draw — so a missing round plate silently substitutes a much smaller generic sprite. I confirmed all 22 s4w round plates exist on disk, so this is not firing today, but it is the classic rdy()-fallback trap in this area and any szMul change should be verified against the real art, not the fallback.

### [CONFIRMED] The pixel-glow pass over the boss's energy sections never appears — for the stage-4 boss or for any boss in the game.
- where: game.js:32689
- now: Confirmed, and the case is stronger than reported. drawBossEnergyGlow exists at 24221-24247 under a header comment (24203-24220) that quotes Mike verbatim: "these guys need to get pixel glow effects on their energy sections". It extracts saturated/bright pixels via xartEmissive (7675-7705; the sat>0.42 && val>0.52 test at 7699) and re-burns them additively on a fast and a slow clock. It is unreachable. In drawBoss (32683-32700): 32687 `_bossFade = (_b && _b.dead) ? bossDeathAlpha(_b.dying || 0) : 1;`; bossDeathAlpha (28759-28762) is literally `return T < BOSS_FADE_HOLD ? 1 : 0;` with BOSS_FADE_HOLD=1.8 at 28757, and the comment at 32684-32685 says so ("deliberately binary ... never a dissolve"). 32688 returns when `_bossFade <= 0`; 32689 `if(_bossFade >= 1){drawBossInner();encounterDamageOverlay(_b,false);return;}`. The only calls to drawBossEnergyGlow and drawBossEnergyField in the entire file are at 32693 and 32694, inside the branch that requires 0 < _bossFade < 1 — a value bossDeathAlpha cannot produce. EXTRA NAIL not in the report: even if a fractional value existed, it only exists when `_b.dead` is true, and drawBossEnergyGlow's own first line (24222) is `if(!b||b.dead||b.enter) return;` — so the branch and the function are mutually exclusive by two independent conditions. drawBossEnergyField (24248) is dead for the same reason. The stage-4 boss would be an ideal subject: its default `_animKey` is `s4w_boss_energized_*` (14010) and I confirmed all 12 frames s4w_boss_energized_00..11.png exist in assets/game/stage4_warfare; drawBossEnergyGlow keys off b._animKey at 24224-24226.
- FIX: Carried forward with the line renumbered. In drawBoss, change assets/game.js:32689 from `if(_bossFade >= 1){drawBossInner();encounterDamageOverlay(_b,false);return;}` to also run the glow: `if(_bossFade >= 1){drawBossInner();if(typeof drawBossEnergyGlow==='function'&&boss)drawBossEnergyGlow(boss);if(typeof drawBossEnergyField==='function'&&boss)drawBossEnergyField(boss);encounterDamageOverlay(_b,false);return;}`. Leave 32693-32694 as they are so the dying path is unchanged. No change is needed inside drawBossEnergyGlow — its own `b.dead` guard is correct for the live branch.
- risk: This turns the glow on for EVERY boss at once, not just stage 4 — stages 1-9 all change appearance, which is the drop's stated intent but is a broad visual change to eyeball per stage. xartEmissive caches one canvas per plate key in _emisCache (7674, 7704); an animated boss cycles 12 frames per reel, so expect ~12-36 cached canvases per boss on first sight and a one-frame hitch the first time each new frame is glowed. It is guarded against tainted-canvas throws at 7687-7692, which on a file:// build silently yields no glow rather than an error — so a local test may show nothing and that is not proof the fix failed.

### [CONFIRMED] The stage-4 boss gives no wind-up or warning before it attacks — no anticipation pose, no pre-flash, no charge cue for most of its weapons.
- where: game.js:13942
- now: Confirmed end to end. shipBossQueueAttack (12904-12933) is the only thing that creates `b._sba` (12917) — the record shipBossVisualPose (12962-12976) reads for the anticipation pulse (12968-12971), and the same function fires the pre-volley muzzle flash (12928) and bossWeaponCharge (12931). Its only two call sites are 29269 and 18654, both immediately preceded by `if(b.fireCd<=0)`. stage4WarfareBossTick sets `b.fireCd=999` on its FIRST line every frame (13942), plus once at init (13434), and shipBossManoeuvre returns early through `if(b._s4war&&typeof stage4WarfareTick==='function')return stage4WarfareTick(b,dt);` at 15280 — so 29269's `b.fireCd -= dt*...` can never bring 999 to zero and _sba is never created. shipBossActionTick IS still called at 15279, one line before that return, but it exits at its `const A=b._sba; if(!A) return;` (12952) so none of the tell/kick machinery engages. That also makes the profile row at 12845 and the slot list at 12805 dead for this boss. What the boss DOES have: entering 'lightning' plays bossWeaponCharge (13776) and runs the `s4w_boss_charge_*` reel for the first 1.18 s (14008) ahead of the first bolt at S.t>=1.12 (13989); the helper turrets play bossWeaponCharge once per windup (13578). Everything else releases cold — burst chaingun 13965-13972, shield orbs 13953-13960, flyaway orbs 13977-13980. Both comparison telegraphs are real: carrierThunderheadStart (17147-17156) sets `tell:.66` at 17151, calls floatText with 'THUNDERHEAD!' at 17153 and bossPhase at 17154, and carrierThunderheadDraw (17177-17188) paints the threatened lanes and strokes the safe gap while `T.t<T.tell`; and the Stage 2/3 sign is l23WarnSymbolDraw (14389) blinking the authored `nwarn_yield` -> `nwarn_alert` plates (14392) over L23_WARN_T=3.00 (14385) with l23WarnSound (14415), clamped by L23_WARN_MINY=46 (14387).
- FIX: Carried forward with lines renumbered. Add a tell window to the mode machine rather than reviving _sba: in stage4WarfareSetMode (assets/game.js:13773) set `S.tell=(mode==='burst'?.45:mode==='flyaway'?.40:0)` alongside the existing resets and change the `S.shot=-.02` seed on that same line to `S.shot=S.tell`; then in stage4WarfareDrawOver (14025) draw `nwarn_yield`/`nwarn_alert` above the hull while `S.t<S.tell`, copying the blink/flip logic of l23WarnSymbolDraw (14389-14413) including its `L23_WARN_MINY`=46 floor (14387, applied at 14410) so the sign clears the boss health gauge.
- risk: Delaying the first shot of each mode by 0.40-0.45 s lowers the boss's DPS; the modes exit on fixed S.t thresholds (13973/13981/13986/14005) so a tell eats into that mode's firing time rather than lengthening the cycle. The warning sign must be clamped exactly like l23WarnSymbolDraw or it draws off-screen or under the boss gauge — the comments at 14397-14410 and again at 54866 record both of those failures happening before, and specifically that L23_WARN_MINY=46 is still required because the boss gauge draws later in the frame.

### [CONFIRMED] The engine's generic boss telegraph ring is inert: nothing can ever set it, and its function has been silently overwritten.
- where: game.js:29362
- now: Confirmed, and I verified the scoping claim rather than assuming it. Two global `function bossTelegraph` declarations exist: the 5-arg ring setter `bossTelegraph(b, x, y, dur, col)` at 29362-29365, whose body assigns `b._tel` at 29364, and the mech-boss draw routine `bossTelegraph(b,K)` at 47227. I ran a string/comment/regex-aware brace-depth scan over the whole file (final depth 0, so the scanner balances): depth at line 29362 is 0 and depth at line 47227 is 0, i.e. both are top-level script declarations in the same scope, not nested in the IIFEs at 12738/58085. Function declarations hoist and the later wins, so the 5-arg version is unreachable by name for the entire program. It also has zero callers: `grep -n bossTelegraph` returns only the two definitions (29362, 47227), the unrelated bossTelegraphDraw definition and call (29366, 32704), and the single 2-arg call `bossTelegraph(b,K)` at 48090. Consequently `b._tel` is written nowhere — the only assignment in the file is 29364, inside the shadowed function — and bossTelegraphDraw (29366-29380), which drawBossInner calls every frame at 32704, returns immediately at 29368 (`if(!b || !b._tel) return`) for every boss in the game. Any attempt to telegraph the stage-4 boss with bossTelegraph(boss,x,y,dur,col) would land on the mech version, evaluate `K.fight` on a number, return at 47228, and silently no-op. I also ran the grep the report's own risk note asks for: there is no `window['bossTelegraph']` or bracketed dynamic lookup anywhere in the file.
- FIX: Carried forward with lines renumbered. Rename the ring setter at assets/game.js:29362 to a unique name (e.g. `bossTelegraphRing`) so it stops being shadowed by the mech-boss draw at 47227, then call that name from wherever a telegraph is wanted. bossTelegraphDraw (29366) needs no change — it reads `b._tel` and starts working as soon as something can set it. Note this fix only makes the ring settable; it does not by itself telegraph the stage-4 boss, which still needs the tell window from the previous finding.
- risk: Low in isolation, since nothing calls the 5-arg form today — renaming it cannot break a live caller. The one thing to watch is that the mech-boss path at 48090 keeps resolving to 47227; leave that definition's name untouched. If the ring is later wired up for real, remember bossTelegraphDraw only ever reads the global `boss` (29367), not subBoss, so a telegraph set on a sub-boss will never draw.

### [ADJUSTED] Stage 5's entry decelerates to a dead stop right before the ship converts. Mike: "DO NOT STOP SCROLLING THE level or speed."

The brake body at 54210 is `drawLaunch._pt+=dt; const p=clamp(drawLaunch._pt/2.0,0,1); drawLaunch._spd=lerp(1750,0,_ease(p));` - a full 2.0 s ramp that terminates at exactly 0. `_spdCap` is 3200 for space stages (54194) and run accelerates at 2600 px/s2 (54196), so `_spd` is pinned at 3200 when brake starts and the FIRST brake frame drops it to ~1750 (`_ease(~0.008)~=0`) - a visible slam - then eases to zero. Stage 6 is the documented exemption from this exact beat (comments at 54203-54204 and 54241-54247, drop 0903x); stage 5 never got the same exemption. `_ease` is at 53856, `lerp` at 6120, `LAUNCH_COUNTDOWN_SCROLL=24` at 54078.

Starting the lerp at `_spdCap` also removes the 3200->1750 first-frame snap. Do not delete the brake phase - the ship-pose branch at 54328-54330 and launchConnDy's 'brake' case at 54073 both key off it.
- risk: launchConnDy() (54069-54077) returns raw join distance during 'run'/'brake' and only eases to 0 during 'settle'. Keeping brake but ending it at cruise is safe because _dist (15240) already exceeds LAND (14400) at the end of run, so raw is 0 throughout brake. Skipping brake outright would drop the brake SFX cue (54201) and the banked-thruster pose at 54328.

### [CONFIRMED] The space background never "tiles up" over the stage - it arrives as a cross-fade, which an earlier drop explicitly ruled out.
- where: game.js:54122
- now: CONFIRMED in full at corrected lines (+15 from the report). stage5SkyToSpaceDraw (54109-54136) builds space as a clipped band whose leading edge is `const yb=(stage5SkyToSpaceDraw._at==null)?0:clamp((scroll||0)-stage5SkyToSpaceDraw._at,0,VH);` (54122), with `_at` latched the first frame progress goes positive (54120-54121). stage5SpaceAscentProgress returns 0 for the whole 'drift' phase (54101) and only goes positive at the start of 'charge' (54102), so the band starts sweeping inside the gravity beat where `scroll` is drawLaunch._bgScroll (call site 54296) advancing at 24 px/s. VH=512 (line 14), so crossing takes 512/24 = 21.3 s while only ~7.84 s of gravity remain: the band reaches ~188 px of a 512 px screen and never completes. Line 54134 then paints the level over the top with `globalAlpha=clamp((p-.72)/.28,0,1)` - a cross-fade, which the block's own comment at 54111-54119 says Mike ruled out ("theres no fading anymore with the stage 5 transition"). Separately, the band tiles at `scroll*0.42` with dh=max(VH,ih*VW/iw)=2556 (54128), while gameplay's _loopDraw (4621-4629) maps the same plate by `mapScroll % 3621` at rate 1.0 - two different samplings of one image, so the stars change rate and offset at the handoff.
- FIX: No change here if the two speed findings are fixed - at 120-260 px/s the band crosses 512 px in 2-4 s and completes long before p reaches .72, leaving the alpha ramp at 54134 as a no-op tail. If the sweep must lead the fusion flash, latch _at earlier by making stage5SpaceAscentProgress return a small ramp during 'drift' (line 54101 `if(p==='drift')return 0;`) rather than touching the band math at 54122.
- risk: Latching _at during 'drift' starts space arriving before the kit does, which the comment at 54099-54100 deliberately prevents ("The horizon does not disappear before the kit arrives"). Prefer fixing speed and leaving the ascent curve alone. Separately, the 0.42 tile rate and the max(VH, ih*sc) scaling at 54128 do not match gameplay's _loopDraw mapping at 4621-4629, so even a completed sweep will show a rate/offset jump at GO unless that is reconciled.

### [ADJUSTED] On a cold cache the "space" that sweeps in during the stage-5 transition is a flat starless black rectangle instead of the tiled space plate.
- where: game.js:25258
- now: CONFIRMED, and it is the classic rdy()-gate-with-a-different-fallback caused upstream by a warm aimed at the wrong stage. beginStage calls `warmStage(arguments[0])` at line 25258, but `run.stage=num` is not set until 25287. warmStage's `add(cfg.master)` at 24720 reads `_levelCfg(n)` at 24718 - and _levelCfg takes NO parameter and switches on run.stage (definition at 3256-3257: `function _levelCfg(){ switch(run.stage){`), the trap documented verbatim at 819 ("_levelCfg() IGNORES ITS ARGUMENT"). So entering stage 5 from stage 4 re-queues bg_stage04_master and never queues bg_stage05_loop. Nothing else catches it: its manifest path is assets/game/bg_stage05_loop.png so the folder regex at 24714 (`levels/level5/|stages/5/`) misses it; the _F prefix table's stage-5 row at 24788 does not name it, while stage 6's row at 24789 DOES name its master `stage6_blue_master` and stage 9's row at 24792 names `nst9_starfield` - stage 5 is the only one of the three left uncovered; and it fails the boot PRELOAD regex at 1908. Consequences are exactly as described: stageLoadReady(5) (24626) reports done without the master, so the 'load' gate at 54251 releases early; stage5SkyToSpaceDraw gates the tile on `XART.rdy('bg_stage05_loop')` at 54126 with a `ctx.fillStyle='#020610'; ctx.fillRect(0,0,VW,yb);` fill at 54125 immediately before it - a plain near-black band; and the outbound 4->5 leg's space band calls s45Tile at line 970, which returns false when not rdy (line 901) and draws nothing, leaving seqCfg(4).fill (outboundDraw, line 1076) showing where space should be.
- FIX: Minimal and lowest-blast-radius: add 'bg_stage05_loop' to the stage-5 row of the _F prefix table at line 24788, exactly as stage 6 lists 'stage6_blue_master' at 24789 and stage 9 lists 'nst9_starfield' at 24792. The addPrefix loop at 24794 will then queue it into stageLoadBegin so stageLoadReady(5) gates on it. The root fix - moving `warmStage(arguments[0])` from 25258 to just after `run.stage=num` at 25287, or giving _levelCfg a real parameter - is correct but far wider.
- risk: Moving the warmStage call changes what every stage preloads (today each stage effectively warms its predecessor's master), so masters that currently arrive by luck could regress - stages 1-9 entry would all need re-verification. Adding the key to _F[5] is inert by comparison but leaves the _levelCfg(n) argument bug in place for every other stage; the note at 819 shows it has already been found once and left. Note also that queuing a 5.1 MiB decode into the stage-5 gate lengthens the 'load' phase at 54246-54251, which at 24 px/s is exactly the crawl finding 2 describes - land this together with the cruise fix, not before it.
